13. Benchmarks: two cap-weighted synthetic TR indices (`IDX:KOSPI_TR`, `IDX:SPX_TR`; built only inside the synthetic data) → `instruments(asset_class=index)` + `bars`.

`EventSpec` (event injection): a dict that uses the column names of a TDS dataset (`corporate_actions`, `delistings`, `fundamentals_events`/`fundamentals_state`, `tradability_daily`) as they are, such as `{"kind": "split", "instrument_id": "KR:T00001", "ex_date": "2020-02-03", "ratio_new_per_old": 2.0}`, plus a `"dataset"` key. When there is an injected event, no random event is made on the same date. The generator's output must be byte-identical for the same arguments (test `test_synth_deterministic`).

`python -m nexusbt.testing.synth --preset P --out DIR [--markets KR,US,ZZ] [--degrade a,b] --validate` runs `tds check --level full` after generating and prints the row counts per dataset, the package `value_sha256` and `semantic_sha256`, and the three profile results.

---

## 5. Normative semantics

### 5.1 Time and "as of"

#### 5.1.1 Representation of time [11.4.1]
- Every time inside the engine is UTC, as integer microseconds (`datetime64[us]`). At the Python boundary it is a tz-aware `datetime(UTC)`. The local date (`session_date`) is a label.
- The times of session s are only `ts_open(s)` and `ts_close(s)` of `calendar_sessions` (the engine does not compute time-zone rules).

#### 5.1.2 The one rule of availability [C1, R-17, INV-01]
- An observation x can be used at decision d only when `ts_available(x) <= decision_ts(d)`. No exceptions (no month-end exception; a currency does not change the time) [11.4.1, Q10].
- **The reference time of market-axis values** [RV-C02]: row s of market m freezes at the **freeze time** `ready_m(s) = ts_close(s) + Δ_m`. `Δ_m` is a constant set by the data: 0 when the bars' availability rule is `ts_end`, that duration when it is `ts_end+<duration>`, and the dataset's `params.ready_delay` when it is a column rule (`{"column": "ts_available"}`) (TDS v1.1; 0 when absent). The term values of row s use only information usable at `cutoff(s) = ready_m(s)`. An instrument whose bar has `ts_available > ready_m(s)` is missing in row s (NaN, counted in the diagnostic `LateBar`) and enters the window from the next row on — the values of a row that has already frozen are not changed by data that arrives late. When decision d reads values of market m, it uses the latest row `s = row_m(d)` with `ready_m(s) <= decision_ts(d)`. Because `ready` is a declared constant and not a data value, deleting the data after τ does not change the row choice (INV-02). Since `decision_ts >= cutoff(s)`, INV-01 holds.
- So financials newly published between the decision time and the row time (for example the US row of the previous day in a decision at the Korean close) are not used. This is conservative and does not look into the future [A-02].

#### 5.1.3 Decision time: the reference clock and sleeves [9.0 Q23, R-62, Q1]

| `clock` | Calendar of the decision schedule | `decision_ts` | Row each sleeve reads |
|---|---|---|---|
| `"<M>_close"` (multi-market default = the first market when the snapshot's `markets` keys are sorted by UTF-8 bytes — it does not rely on JSON object order, and the resolved value is fixed in the manifest; `KR_close` for KR+US) [R3-18] | the calendar of market M | `ts_close(M, d) + Δ_M` | M: d. Another market m: the last session with `ready_m(s) <= decision_ts` (for example, at `KR_close` US is usually d−1, at `US_close` KR is usually the same day d) |
| `market_local` | each sleeve its own calendar | `ready(d)` of that sleeve's market | its own market's d |

- A strategy with only one market is `market_local` whatever the `clock` (no notice).
- Each decision builds a `DecisionContext`:

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class DecisionContext:
    decision_id: str            # f"{sleeve_id}:{session_date:%Y%m%d}:{clock}"
    snapshot_id: str
    snapshot_semantic_hash: str  # TDS 4.5 semantic hash [RV-C10]
    sleeve_id: str              # market key (TDS markets)
    calendar_id: str            # schedule calendar
    session_date: date          # session date of the schedule calendar
    decision_ts: datetime       # UTC
    clock: str
    availability_policy: str    # tds.json policies.availability_default (+ per-dataset rules in the manifest)
    scheduled: bool             # is the decision on the schedule (False for a screen preview)
    anchor_decision_id: str | None  # starting point of the target-state recursion (None when the recursion is not used) [SC-02]
```

- **Execution session**: an order from decision d can first fill in the first session of that sleeve's market with `ts_open > decision_ts` [11.4.1]. At `KR_close` the US sleeve fills at the US open of the same date, and **when there is no cross-market term** this is the same information and the same fill as `market_local`'s "US d−1 close decision → fill at the d open". The two modes differ in the schedule calendar (whose market's month end is the month end) and in the information of cross-market terms (`across_markets`, the exchange-rate time of `.to(ccy)`).
- Even when the decision schedule falls on a holiday of that sleeve's market (for example `KR_close`, a Korean holiday on a day the US trades), the decision follows the schedule calendar. The US sleeve decides only on Korean session dates.

#### 5.1.4 Date-only publication [11.4.1]
An observation with only `known_on = k` (a date) has `ts_available = ts_open(the first session after k in that market)` (TDS rule `next_session_open(known_on)`). It is used from that session's close decision on. A TDS package gives this value as a column or a rule (4.6), and the engine applies the same rule to external data (`from_parquet`) too.

#### 5.1.5 Availability rules per data

| Data | Value used at a decision |
|---|---|
| bars (`bars`, freq=1d) | the rows up to `row_m(d)` (freeze time `ready_m`, 5.1.2). The window goes back from that row (bar availability time default = `ts_end` = `ts_close`) |
| `share_counts` | among the rows with `ts_available <= cutoff(s)`, the one with the latest `as_of_date` (on a tie, the later `ts_available`), the first `count_kind` present in the order of `[data] share_count_kinds` (5.3.5a) |
| `fundamentals_state` | the one interval with `ts_known_from <= cutoff(s) < ts_known_to` (5.3.5) |
| `feature_panels` | the latest `period` with `ts_available <= cutoff(s)` (a month's panel cannot be used in that month's month-end decision) [Q10, C1] |
| `classifications`, `universe_membership`, `instrument_symbols` | two-time-axis rectangular lookup (5.3.6): `ts_known_from <= cutoff(s) < ts_known_to` and `effective_from <= session_date(s) < effective_to` (4.6) |
| `eligibility_daily` | when row s has **that session's row** and `ts_available <= cutoff(s)`, that row's `is_eligible`, otherwise false (no carry-over; with the TDS v1.1 default availability rule `session_close`, from that day's close decision on) [RV-C09]; under the fallback rule, the last published `feature_panels.is_investable` |
| `corporate_actions` | only what the resolver (5.3.3a) resolved at `cutoff(s)` (an event is visible from its existence time). Events with unpublished terms: 5.3.4 [R3-01] |
| account inputs (valuation prices, receivables, terminal recoveries) | only values with `ts_available <= decision_ts` are used in decision sizing (5.6.9, INV-01) [RV-C01] |
| `fx_rates` | decision inputs (`.to()`, funding wait): the last observation with `ts_available <= decision_ts`; beyond `max_stale_days_decision`, `.to()` is NaN + a warning, and the funding wait is `FxStaleError` (error E20). Valuation for reports: `ts_event <= valuation time` (after the fact allowed, not a decision input) [11.5] |
| external data | `known_on` → 5.1.4 |
| market-level series `Market.series(id)` | up to `row_{m'}(d)` of that instrument's market m' (the same `ready` as bars). If m' = the sleeve market, the same market axis; otherwise gathered at the decision (5.3.2 (6)) [V31-13] |
| macro series `Macro.series(id)` | among the vintages with `ts_available ≤ decision_ts`, the last observation period (vintage rule 5.3.12); the end of the observation period is not the availability time [R4F-EV-01] |
| events `Events.*` | `ts_available` (for corporate actions `ts_announced`/`ts_terms_available`, for filings the receipt time) ≤ `decision_ts`; events before the instrument's first session are ignored [R4F-EV-02] |
| `Trad.*` | `availability_by_column`: prior information `session_open` (from that session's decision on), the day's outcome `session_close` [V31-30] |
| `Label.*` | forbidden as a decision input [R-16] |

#### 5.1.6 Aligning start and end [11.4.1]
`start` is aligned forward on the schedule calendar (the first session on or after that date), `end` backward (the last session on or before that date). The run period is the intersection with the segment (6.1). There is no forced liquidation at the end (5.6.14).

### 5.2 Summary of decision and execution

```
session t (market m), phase order session_start < open < intraday < close_fill < close_mark < decision < session_end  [RV-C02]
 ts_open(t) ──► [session_start: resolver effects · payments · terminations] ─► [open: sells → buys → new exit orders] ─► [intraday: sells → buys (intraday sale proceeds not used) → new exit orders (adverse side only)]
            ─► [close_fill: market at=close, sells → buys] ─► [close_mark: close valuation · trailing · expiry] ─► ts_close(t)
                                         │
                       decision_ts(d) = (clock rule, ready included) ─► signal computation → canonical target → order intent (fixed share quantity, only knowable account values)
                                                          → cancel and replace remaining rebalancing orders → valid from the next open ─► [session_end]
```

### 5.3 Pipeline computation

#### 5.3.1 Market axis and blocks [D2, design 4.4.2]
- Per market a dense block `X[m]: (number of sessions, number of instruments) float64`. Instrument axis = the instruments with `asset_class = equity ∧ is_synthetic = false` listed at least once in that chunk's period (sorted by `instrument_id`; index and synthetic instruments are not put in the block and go only through the benchmark path, 3.3) [RV-Q06]. Cells before listing and after delisting are "absent" (value NaN, existence mask False).
- Chunk = a session range (by default a year; smaller than 1 year when the memory budget is short; 5.3.8). The tail `W_max − 1` rows of the previous chunk are read as an overlap.
- A block builds the `instrument_id → column number` and `session_ord → row number` mappings in memory. Since the files have no integer keys, the Parquet dictionary index is turned into block column numbers once (a small table the size of the dictionary), and `session_date` gets its row number with `searchsorted` [A-05].

#### 5.3.2 Plan [R-14, 11.7]
1. From the decision grid (schedule × sleeve), find for each market the set of rows needed, `R_m = {row_m(d)}`.
2. Follow the graph from top to bottom, propagating the row set each term needs: if a window-W term needs row set R, its input is `⋃_{r∈R} [r−W+1, r]`; for `shift(k)`, `R − k`. Cross-sectional operations use the same row set [11.7].
3. Pruning: each term computes only the rows it needs. When a daily cross-sectional term is used by a window below it, every daily row that window covers is computed [design 4.4.1, 11.7].
4. Each column is read once with its longest window, and shorter windows use the tail of the same array [design 4.4.2].
5. Terms with the same structural hash share one node. A node is dropped when its reference count reaches 0.
6. `across_markets=True` terms and `Market.series` terms of another market [V31-13]: at each decision the `row_m(d)` values of the markets are gathered and computed. The result is a value on the decision grid and does not return to the market axis. Using such a term as a window or `shift` input is a `TermDomainError` (error E17) when the graph is built.

#### 5.3.3 Adjustment [D3, R-18, C2, INV-11]
Definitions: the price multiplier `m_e` of effective session e = the price factor the resolver (5.3.3a) gave for that event (source TDS `corporate_actions.price_factor`, formula TDS 5.11), `C[x] = Π_{e ≤ x} m_e` (forward cumulative, `C = 1` on the first listed session; the same meaning as TDS `cum_factor_*` [TDS-D9]). The split factor `C_split` is the product over `affects = split_and_total` events only, and the total factor `C_total` the product over all events (`affects ≠ none`). The quantity factor `Q[x] = Π_{e ≤ x} q_e` (`q_e` = the resolver's quantity effect `share_factor`; used for volume, share counts and holding quantities). Base price `B[s] = raw[s] / C[s]`. The package's `cum_factor_*` is used as a shortcut only when it equals the resolver's result (check `TDS-A001` guarantees agreement with the latest resolved version); when absent, it is built from the events (4.5 F-04). Events with a null `price_factor`: the rule at the end of 4.6.

- **Reference meaning (ground truth)**: the value at s ≤ t in the window of computation day t = `raw[s] · Π_{s < e ≤ t, e available} m_e`. When every event up to t is available, `= raw[s]·C[t]/C[s] = B[s]·C[t]`.
- **Reference implementation** (`compute._kernels_ref.adjusted_window`): for each row t, the window `B[t−W+1..t] · C[t]` (prices, split or total factor), and volume `vol[s]·Q[t]/Q[s]`. For cells with a pending event of 5.3.4 or an event whose resolved version differs by reference time (amendments, cancellations), the product is computed directly from the resolver's event list. User window functions always receive this window.
- **Registered-kernel shortcuts**: a kernel declares its adjustment class (the table of 3.4).
  - Homogeneous of degree 0: the result computed on `B` (or the volume version of `B`) = the ground truth. No multiplication per row.
  - Homogeneous of degree 1: row t of the result computed on `B` is multiplied by `C[t]` (for volume, `1/C_split[t]`).
  - Invariant: computed on raw values.
  - Anything else (thresholds, nonlinear mixing, price-level ranks): no shortcut. Computed on the reference path.
- **Proof obligation**: for each registered kernel, the P-ADJ property test (random events, random per-instrument scalars) checks shortcut = reference implementation (relative 1e-12; an algebraically equal transform is not promised to be bit-identical). A kernel that does not pass cannot be registered [RV-C11].

#### 5.3.3a Corporate-action resolver (resolver) [RV-C03, TDS v1.1 5.11]

Adjustment (5.3.3, 5.3.4), share counts (5.3.5a), accounting (5.6.10~5.6.13), ground truth (5.8) and checks (A001) all read events through the one `Snapshot.actions(market, cutoff) -> ResolvedActions`. The resolver is a pure function, and the same `(snapshot semantic hash, market, cutoff)` gives the same result.

1. **Chains**: events are tied into chains by following `revision_of` (the root of a chain = the event without `revision_of`, chain id = the root's `event_id`). A cycle is TDS-A007 (E14).
2. **The version at a reference time** [R3-01]: the **existence time** of version r, `ts_known(r)` = the earliest non-null value of `ts_announced` and `ts_terms_available` (`ts_effective` when both are null — the 4.5 F-10 rule), and the **terms time** `ts_terms(r) = coalesce(ts_terms_available, ts_known(r))`. The version used at reference time c = among the versions with `ts_known(r) <= c`, the one with the latest `ts_known` (on a tie, the last in ascending `event_id`). When there is no such version, the chain is "unknown" at c. v3.1's `ts_pub = coalesce(ts_terms_available, …)` hid an event with late terms entirely until its terms were published, so the pending branch of 4 and G-20 (an entitlement of unknown amount) could not be reached. `ts_effective` is used only when both other times are missing (if an event announced later than its effective time were visible from the effective moment, that would be future information).
3. **Status**: when the chosen version has `status = cancelled`, there is no effect at c. When `announced` and `ts_effective <= c`, no effect + E30 ("effective event still announced", counted). When `effective`, it has effect.
4. **Waiting for terms**: when the chosen version has `ts_terms(r) > c` (existence known but terms unpublished), the price effect waits as in 5.3.4, the entitlement is an **entitlement of unknown amount** (the quantity fixed to the holding at the start of the effective session — the entitlement or obligation remains even if the holding is sold or covered after that), and the quantity effect follows the rule of 6. Amounts already booked from an earlier version are left as they are and corrected by the correction entry of 7 in the first session after the terms are published [R3-01].
5. **Four effects** (from the one chosen version):
   - Price factor `m_e`: `price_factor` ("none" when null, 4.6).
   - Quantity factor `q_e`: `share_factor` is used **explicitly** (split r → r, stock dividend b → 1 + b, rights and cash events → 1, a merger extinguishes the old instrument's holding + the new instrument's `ratio_new_per_old`). When `share_factor` is null, it is built by the `ratio_new_per_old` rule (TDS 5.11) only for split-like kinds (`split`, `reverse_split`, `stock_dividend`, `bonus_issue`, `capital_reduction`), and for other kinds it is not inferred as the reciprocal of the price factor (in that case the quantity effect is "none" → an unsupported event if held) [RV-C03].
   - Entitlement: cash distribution (`cash_per_share`, currency, `gross_or_net`), the new instrument and ratio to be received.
   - Settlement: `pay_date` (`pay_date_status`), the estimation rule (5.6.11).
6. **Timing of effects**: price and quantity effects from `ts_effective` (the start of effective session e). But when the terms of a quantity-changing event (`affects = split_and_total`) are published after it takes effect (TDS-A008 warning), accounting changes the quantity at the start of the first session after the terms are published (orders in between use the old quantity), and the price adjustment goes in from publication on, as in 5.3.4.
7. **Correction of amounts already booked**: when accounting booked a receivable or cash at reference time c1 from version r1, and at a later reference time c2 (> c1) the resolver gives a different version r2 (a different amount or ratio, or `cancelled`), accounting makes a **correction entry** at the start of the first session after c2 (`account_events.kind = action_correction`, amount = r2 effect − r1 effect, in integer minor units; a receivable correction before payment, a cash correction after payment). Past entries are not changed (events are canonical, INV-16).
8. **A001**: the checker calls the same resolver with `c = +∞` (knowledge of the whole snapshot) and compares the `cum_factor_*` ratios (multiplying only the one latest version of each amendment chain; a cancelled chain is 1.0).
9. **Tests**: `tds/tests/test_actions.py::test_resolver_at_cutoffs` (the resolver is `nexusbt_tds.actions`, T0.15a; engine-side consumption is `tests/unit/compute/test_adjust.py` and golden cases) — an amended split ratio, a dividend cancelled after announcement, an amount corrected after payment, a split + cash dividend on the same day, a lapsed right (quantity unchanged), and a missing factor, each at three reference times (before first publication, after the first version, after the amendment); v3.2: a version whose existence is known and whose terms are late (`::test_late_terms_revision_visible_at_ex_date`), an event with an announcement time not visible first at the effective moment (`::test_existence_time_ignores_effective_when_announced`) [R3-01]. Hand-calculated G-43~G-47, a G-20 variant.
10. **Events learned late** [R3-01]: when the `ts_known` of a chain's first version is after effective session e (an after-the-fact filing), accounting adds the entitlements and obligations for the **holding at the start of e** (recomputed from the canonical events `account_events`) at the start of the first session after `ts_known` (past entries are not changed, INV-16). Price adjustment from after `ts_terms`, as in 5.3.4.

#### 5.3.4 Events whose terms are published late [11.4.3 (5), RV-C03]
When the terms time `ts_terms` of the resolved version (5.3.3a) of event e is `> cutoff(t)` [R3-01], the window of row t does not include e (those cells take the reference path). For cash distributions (`affects = total_only`) `close_tr` is affected; for quantity events (`split_and_total`, rare, A008 warning) `close`, `close_tr` and `volume`. The `C` shortcut is used only for cells with "no pending event". The engine builds a pending mask of `(instrument, row)` for each market block. Account handling is 5.6.10~5.6.11.

#### 5.3.5 `Fund.x.latest` [R-63, C3]
- The value of row s = `value·10^scale` of the interval in the edition's `fundamentals_state` with `item = x` and `ts_known_from <= cutoff(s) < ts_known_to`. NaN when there is none.
- When `max_age_days > 0`, a value with `session_date(s) − period_end > max_age_days` is NaN (the same as the 550-day rule of the reference producer's panel).
- Implementation: in `fundamentals_state` the known intervals do not overlap per `(edition, instrument_id, item)` (TDS K5, T003), so it is a one-axis lookup: the `cutoff` array is matched to the array sorted by `ts_known_from` with `searchsorted(side="right") − 1`, then the `ts_known_to` condition is checked. It is not expanded and stored per day [design 4.4.5]. (Two-time-axis tables do not use this method — 5.3.6.)
- In the strict edition, cells without a value are not filled from the compare edition [risk 18].
- Period operations such as `.lag(periods=)` and `.ttm()` are not in the engine. When needed, the producer exports them as resolved items [9.0 Q24, A-06].

#### 5.3.5a Share counts and market capitalisation [TDS-D9, TDS-Q4]
- The raw share count of row s, `n(s)` = `shares` of the row chosen by the 5.1.5 `share_counts` rule (that row's `as_of_date` = a). Applying events: `shares_out_raw(s) = n · Q[s] / Q[a]` (changed by the quantity events that took effect between a and s; `Q` is the resolver's cumulative quantity factor; when that event's terms are published after `cutoff(s)`, it is not included, as in 5.3.4) [RV-C03].
- `Price.shares_out` in the window (as of t) = `shares_out_raw(s) · Q[t] / Q[s]`. `Price.market_cap`(s) = `close_unadjusted[s] · shares_out_raw(s)`.
- The same interval-lookup implementation (sorted array + `searchsorted`), not expanded per day.

#### 5.3.6 Panel, classifications, universe — rectangular lookup [RV-C08]
`Panel` and `share_counts` are one-axis lookups (the 5.1.5 table). `Panel.x.age_days` = `session_date(s) − as_of_date`.

**Two-time-axis tables** (`classifications`, `universe_membership`, `instrument_symbols`) use the **rectangular lookup**. For each identifying key `K` (classifications: `(instrument_id, scheme, level)` + the edition filter; membership: `(universe_id, instrument_id)`; symbols: `(instrument_id, symbol_type)`), the answer to query `(cutoff c, date q)` = the row with `ts_known_from <= c < ts_known_to` **and** `effective_from <= q < effective_to` (the strict edition excludes row-level `backfilled` rows). There must be exactly one (two or more is TDS-T012, E14 in `full` at open; E14 when met during a run that was opened at the `manifest` level), and none means missing. v2's method of choosing the one row with the largest `ts_known_from` and then checking effectiveness is **not used** (a future-effective row announced in advance hides the currently effective row).

Implementation: for each `K`, the rows are kept as an array sorted by `ts_known_from`; for each query c, the candidate rows meeting the known condition (usually 1~3) are gathered with an interval index (the `ts_known_from <= c` prefix + filtering `ts_known_to > c`) and then filtered by the effective condition. It is not expanded per day.

Fixed examples (tests T-18 and T-19, `tests/unit/data/test_intervals.py`):

| # | Rows (effective / known) | Query (c, q) | Answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 announced in advance | A: effective [2019-01-01, ∞) known [2018-12-01, ∞); B: effective [2020-03-20, ∞) known [2020-03-06, ∞), and version A', in which A's `effective_to` changed to 2020-03-20 as known from 2020-03-06 | (2020-03-10, 2020-03-10) | A' (currently effective). B meets the known condition but is not yet effective |
| 2 late discovery | C: effective [2015-06-01, ∞) known [2017-01-01, ∞) | (2016-01-01, 2016-01-01) | none (the old code's row if there is one) |
| 3 amendment | D: effective [2018-01-01, ∞) known [2018-01-02, 2019-05-01); D': the same effective period, only the code differs, known [2019-05-01, ∞) | (2019-06-01, 2018-06-01) | D' (the record believed at that time) |
| 4 level | a gics level 1 row and a level 3 row with the same effective and known periods | (c, q), `level=1` | the one level 1 row (level is in the key, so not a duplicate) |
| 5 edition | strict row E (`edition=strict`) and compare row F (`edition=compare`) with the same effective and known periods | strict run | E (a compare run gets F; a `both` row is in both) |

`history_mode` is in the lookup function's signature (`Snapshot.intervals(..., history_mode="economic")`), and its only v1 value is economic [blueprint chapter 3].

#### 5.3.7 Running user window functions [R-05, design 4.4.3]
- For each row t (only the rows needed after pruning), the input window `(W, N_alive)` is built: from `sliding_window_view(B, W, axis=0)[t]` (no copy), only the alive columns are taken with `np.take` and multiplied by `C[t]` (the product is a new array — the original is safe even if the user modifies it). The set of alive columns is recomputed only at rows where the existence mask changes.
- A return that is not `(N_alive,)` or has a different dtype is a `TermOutputError` (a sub-message of error E8).
- The function must be pure. For a function that reads global arrays or files, the static check leaves an `ImpureFunctionWarning` (error E24) and turns off that node's L1 cache; for constant-like globals (int, float, str, bool, tuple), the value goes into the node hash (6.5, 5.3.9) [RV-C10].

#### 5.3.8 Chunks, parallelism, determinism [R-27, 11.7, INV-03]
- By default 1 computation process, numpy threads `[compute] threads`. Chunk size = the largest session range that does not exceed the memory budget (`memory_budget_gb`, **one budget for the whole process tree**: divided by the number of workers when `workers > 1`): a year is tried first, and when even 1 year exceeds it, it is split smaller into quarters, months, then by halving the session count (the 1-year minimum rule is abolished) [RV-I05]. Estimated bytes = rows × columns × 8 × (maximum number of nodes alive at once + 2). When even one session row exceeds the budget, E18 ("memory_budget_gb too small for {n} instruments").
- `workers > 1` is process parallelism by chunk, and it can be turned on only after the chunk-invariance test (P-CHUNK) passes. Results are joined in chunk order.
- Reductions (sums, means) follow the fixed order of 5.3.10. Numba kernels are `parallel=False` (v1). Random numbers are not used (probes use `[checks] seed`).
- **Sleeve independence and column batching** [V31-11, INV-21]: once the targets (`TargetFrame`) are set, fills and accounting are independent between sleeves (no cash movement between sleeves, 5.6.9). So the runner may batch sleeves, scenarios (κ, delisting lo/hi, capacity multiples, `offsets`) and sweep candidates as **extra columns of the same session loop** (separate state per column, prices and event resolution once) (vectorbt's grouped-column method). The result bits must equal those of separate runs (P-SLEEVE-ISO, P-DET). Exception: a `[taxes] pay = "cash"` batch spanning several markets synchronises every year at the payment session.
- Scenario replays (gross, cost0x, …, capacity) and the execution of sweep candidates are independent of each other, so they can run as parallel processes when `workers > 1`, and results are merged in scenario-name order and `candidate_key` order. Default `workers = 1` (OQ-14).

#### 5.3.9 Cache [R-12, C9, INV-05, SC-15]
There are three layers: **L0** the raw block cache, **L1** the term value cache, **L2** the run outputs (`runs/`, 5.9).
- **L0** (implemented in M6, key rule fixed from M1): dense `float64` matrices per market × column × year (including the existence mask, `obs_status` codes, `C_split`, `C_total` and `Q`), kept as uncompressed Arrow IPC files in `<runs.dir>/.cache/l0/`. Key = sha256(canonical JSON{**the resolution metadata hash of that dataset** (that dataset's `kind`, `schema_version`, `params`, `availability`, `pit_quality`, `key` and column units in `tds.json`, the package `policies`, and only the **stable fields** of `markets` — that market's `calendar_id`, `currency`, `timezone`; the watermark `complete_through*` is not included (it changes every day and made the hit rate 0 in v3.1)), **the file sha256 of the files of that (market, year) partition** (`files[].sha256`; the optional `value_sha256` is at the H004 warning level, so a wrong one does not block, and it is not used as identity), **the corporate-action chain hash of that market** (for every chain with at least one version effective on or before the end of that year, the canonical JSON of `event_id`, `ts_known`, `ts_terms`, `status`, `ts_effective` and the four effects of **every version** — an amendment of a middle version or a correction of only a publication time changes the key too; the `C` and `Q` accumulations and the resolution per reference time depend on this), **the axis datasets** (the file sha256 of that market's `instruments` and `calendar_sessions` — listing periods and sessions set the block's shape), market, column, year, edition, block version `l0-v4`, engine `nexusbt.__version__`}) [RV-C10, V31-12, V31-PUB-03, R3-04]. When the producer reuses an unchanged partition by cloning or hard link (TDS 4.1), the file sha256 is the same, so this key still matches. Even when a new snapshot comes out every day, the L0 of past partitions that did not change still hits (incremental reuse like Qlib's disk cache; unlike Qlib, a past correction changes the file sha256 or the chain hash and misses automatically). Reads are **read-only memory maps**, and user window functions get only copies (5.3.7). Deleting a snapshot does not delete L0 at once (another snapshot may share it through the same file sha256): beyond the `cache_max_gb` limit, entries referenced by no remaining snapshot are deleted first, then the least recently used [R3-04]. Each entry also records the source file identity `(dev, inode, size, mtime_ns)`, checked on open (4.2) [R3-16]. `Snapshot.market_block` hides L0 (no change to the public API).
- **L1** key = sha256(canonical JSON{node structural hash, the input L0 keys — the hash of the L0 keys of every (market, year) partition reached by the **input row set** with windows and `shift` propagated to the end of the graph (5.3.2) (not only the year of the output rows: the 252-session window of a January row depends on the previous year's partition) — and the computed row mask, all the file sha256 of the datasets without year partitions that the node reads (`fundamentals_state`, `classifications`, `fx_rates` and so on) [V31-12, R3-04], external inputs (`from_parquet` file sha256, resolved arguments), market, edition, the resolved query policy (availability rule, `ready_delay`, adjustment policy, the `fx` series choice, `share_count_kinds`), calendar version, `fund.max_age_days`, precision, kernel semantics version (`kernel_semantics`, ADR-0019 A/B), engine `nexusbt.__version__`}). Node structural hash = operator name + parameters + input node hashes + the user function's canonical source + **the captured constant global values** (the rule of 6.5). A node that reads a non-constant global does not use the L1 cache (E24) [RV-C10].
- Value = an Arrow IPC file of the market axis `(row range, computed row mask, instrument_id, value)` (a Filter node stores the value together with the validity mask — a three-valued round trip [R3-13]). When the computed row mask does not cover all the requested rows, it is not a hit (missing rows are not filled) [R3-04]. Chunk placement is not in the key (since chunking does not change results). Writes go to a temporary file → `rename`. When the size exceeds `cache_max_gb`, the least recently used are deleted first. The cache may be deleted at any time. L0 + L1 together do not exceed `cache_max_gb`.

#### 5.3.10 Numeric determinism rules [SC-03, INV-03, INV-04, INV-13]
Registered kernels (built-in factors, operators, cross-sectional operations, statistics) follow the rules below. User window functions are required only to give "same window input → same output" (INV-03).
The bit promise of registered kernels is about chunking, permutation, caching and reruns in **the same environment** (same `uv.lock`, same CPU kind and OS). There is no bit promise between different environments (the tolerance is set by measurement, using the 8.1 L2 criteria) [RV-C11].

1. **Window kernels**: output row t is computed only from the values of window `[t−W+1, t]`, as a left fold in ascending row order (`acc = acc + x[r]`, float64) (**ADR-0019 definition A**). Cumulative-sum differences across chunk boundaries (`cumsum[t] − cumsum[t−W]`) and running sums carried from the previous row (add and subtract) are not used (rounding would depend on the chunk start and break INV-03, and rounding traces of observations outside the window would remain and break "a function of the values in the window only" (INV-14)). **The pre-registered alternative definition B** (block partial sums): partial sums of blocks fixed by the absolute session number (`session_ord // 64`) are built as left folds, and a window = the left fold of the two end pieces + the left fold of the inner block sums. It uses only values in the window and anchors to absolute numbers, so chunk and start-date invariance hold bit for bit. If in the M1 bench (T1.24) the computation of all daily rows of `SMA(252)` exceeds the stage budget, it switches to B by ADR (a `kernel_semantics` version bump → the L1 key changes, only the P-KERNEL expected values change) [RV-D1].
2. **Fast window kernels** allow only methods bit-identical (in the same environment) to the reference definition: refolding each window (Numba O(W)), block partial sums in the same association order if definition B is adopted, integer counts, and monotone queues for minimum and maximum. O(1) updates of sums, means and variances are not used. For variance, the two-pass definition (mean first, then the sum of squared deviations) is the reference.
2a. **Numeric classes** [RV-C11]: the arithmetic class (+ − × ÷ sqrt, min/max, comparisons) is bit-identical in the same environment. For the transcendental class (`log`, `log1p`, `exp`, `pow`), the reference and fast paths use **the same compiled element function** (`nx.compute._elem`, compiled once with Numba; the reference path too calls the compiled version, not `.py_func`), so it is bit-identical in the same environment and relative 1e-12 across environments. Power weights (`decay^k` of `EWMA`) are constant arrays outside the kernel.
3. **Cross-sectional reductions** (mean, standard deviation, sum, quantiles) gather values in **sorted `instrument_id` order**, not memory column order, and then left-fold (sorted index; ADR-0019) [OQ-5]. The exact sum (`math.fsum`) is used only for comparison in reference tests.
4. The reference implementation does not rely on the pairwise summation order of `np.sum`, `np.mean`, `np.nanmean` or `np.add.reduce` (the order changes with array length and alignment). It is written as an explicitly ordered loop (Numba `py_func` or pure Python).
5. Numba uses `fastmath=False`, `parallel=False`, `error_model="numpy"`. Operations that call BLAS (matrix multiplication, 2-D `np.dot`, `lstsq`) are not used in v1 kernels (the bits change with the BLAS thread count and implementation).
6. The later optimizer output (`nx.portfolio.optimize`, reserved) is an exception to bit determinism: the solver name, version and tolerance are written in the manifest, weights are rounded at 1e-10 and small negatives set to 0, and a solver failure is a failure (not replaced by equal weights). Comparisons are bit-exact only with the same solver version on the same machine; otherwise within tolerance [SC-22].
7. Tests: P-KERNEL (fast = reference, bit-identical in the same environment; for the transcendental class, a check that the same element function is used), P-CHUNK (including chunks smaller than 1 year), P-PERM (shuffling memory column order), P-START (INV-14).
8. **Swappable implementations** [R3-26]: other implementations of window and cross-sectional kernels (Numba fast kernels, a Rust crate and so on, the 2.4 seam) keep the definitions of this section (summation order, element functions) as they are and must be bit-identical to the reference in the same environment (`test_kernel_swap_bit_equal`).

#### 5.3.11 ML factor node (`nx.ml.factor`) [R4-23, user decision 2026-10-28]
- **Place**: the registered special Pipeline node `MLFactor`. Input = feature nodes (Factors with guaranteed timing) + a label node (`label`: `"fwd_return"`, `"fwd_return_adj"`, `Label.*`, `labels.triple_barrier(...)`). Output = predictions per decision × instrument (float64), used like an ordinary Factor (`.rank(mask=)`, `signal=`, `nx.evaluate(pred)` — counted as a factor candidate). It is not a window-style user function (the engine manages its state, so it is not subject to the start-date probe E32).
- **Samples**: for each decision d, the rows sorted by (d, `instrument_id`) for which the universe (for a strategy its `universe`, for evaluate `universe=`) is true and all features are finite. Missing features are not filled (INV-12; the user uses `fillna` if needed).
- **Refit schedule**: `refit` (a Schedule, anchored to the calendar, INV-14). Refit time τ_k = the decision time of that session. Predictions use that fit's result for decisions with `τ_k ≤ decision_ts < τ_{k+1}`. Predictions for decisions before the first fit are NaN and are counted in `skips` (reason `ml_no_model`).
- **Training window rule (enforced by the engine)**: the training samples at τ = the (d, i) for which (a) d is in the window — for `train=nx.ml.rolling("5y")`, `decision_ts(d) ∈ (τ − 5y, τ]`; for `nx.ml.expanding(min_length="10y")`, from the first decision in the snapshot where the features are finite up to τ (the first fit after the length 10y is filled) — (b) the `ts_close` of the session numbered (the number of the market session containing `max(ts_label_end, ts_label_available)` (5.8)) + `embargo` is ≤ τ (when that time falls between sessions, counting starts from the next session; the numbers of G-92 follow this formula) [R4F-34], (c) the label is finite. A gap as long as the label period arises by itself, and the user does not compute a shift. The window is set by the snapshot data, not by the run's start date (decisions before the run start can be training samples too — so moving the start date leaves the fit at the same τ the same, INV-14).
- **E2 ports and E29**: a label node is accepted only at two ports, `evaluate(target=)` and `ml.factor(label=)`. A label that passed this port is used only by the rule above, and the prediction output does not propagate the label marking; reaching it by another path is E2 as now. **Training is always forward-only** (user decision 2026-10-28 [R4F-03]): in any validation method (walk-forward, CPCV, CSCV, segments), the training samples at τ follow only rules (a)~(c) above — there are no samples after τ (the same shape as live operation; AFML-style "training after the test block" is not used for ML training). In addition, (d) in CPCV, the decisions in that split's **test blocks** (including those before τ), the decisions whose label interval overlaps a test block (purging), and the decisions within `embargo` sessions after the end of a test block (embargo) are removed — this is where the splitter's `embargo` has meaning in ML. Walk-forward does **not** cut the window to the fold's training range (a `refit` inside the test window uses the samples up to that time = a simulation of operation). A setting under which samples overlapping the test block's labels could enter (for example a user `Label.*` that purging cannot stop because `embargo` is shorter than the label period) is E29 before the run — v3.2's reserved check 8 (6.5) becomes concrete with this rule. The report's leakage ledger has one line `ml fits: forward-only (no samples after refit time)`. The two-sided training option (`ml_two_sided`) is v1.x (12.1).
- **Model contract**: `fit(X, y, sample_weight=None)`, `predict(X)`, `get_params()`, `set_params(**p)`; classification is binary only: `predict_proba(X)[:, -1]` = the probability of the larger (positive) of the sorted labels is the prediction; three or more classes is E16 [R4F-32]. Label selectors: `label=nx.labels.triple_barrier(...).ret` (default, regression), `.side` (classification), and `evaluate(target=)` takes the same selectors [R4F-33]. Each fit makes a new object with `type(model)(**model.get_params())` (no state leaks from an earlier fit). The engine does not import scikit-learn (the optional dependency `nexusbt[ml]` is for examples and tests).
- **Pretrained models** [R5-03, user decision 2026-10-28]: a model that only predicts without training (time-series foundation models such as TimesFM, Chronos and Moirai, models trained outside) is wrapped with `nx.ml.pretrained(model, *, training_cutoff: str, corpus: str, allow_overlap: bool = False)` and put into `nx.ml.factor(model=...)` (it need not have `fit`; the engine calls only `predict`). **Signature rule** [checked by Sol]: a trained model requires `label`, `train` and `refit` (E16 when missing), and a pretrained model must have all three as `None` (E16 "pretrained model is not trained by the engine" when given); a pretrained model's predictions can still be scored with `evaluate(target=)`. **`training_cutoff` is required** (the last date of the pretraining data; E16 "pretrained model needs training_cutoff" when absent): if the pretraining data contains the test period, that is future information. **Boundary time** [checked by Sol]: take the last session s₀ on or before the `training_cutoff` date in the schedule calendar of the sleeve market, and the `ts_close` of the `embargo`-th session after s₀ is the boundary B (the `ts_close` of s₀ when `embargo` = 0). If there is even one decision with `decision_ts ≤ B`, the run is **blocked** (E48 `PretrainedLeakError`, failing before the run, showing the number of blocked decisions and the first and last dates). The boundary time itself is blocked too. With `allow_overlap=True` it is not blocked: E49 warning + the `pretrained_overlap` marking on those decisions + the report header line "pretrained model {name} saw data up to {cutoff}; {n} decisions before it are marked" and the manifest `assumptions`. In validation (6.3a), overlapping paths and folds are marked separately in the distribution. The model identity (name, version, `training_cutoff`, `corpus`) goes into both `fit_id` and **the strategy's `graph_hash`**, so a change of version, cutoff date or data counts as a new candidate (6.2). `allow_overlap=True` is **an explicit exception to INV-01**: declared by the user, allowed only with the manifest `assumptions` and the marking, and never on by default.
- **Determinism**: (1) seed = `int.from_bytes(sha256(f"{run_seed}:{fit_id}")[:4], "big")`, put in when `get_params()` has `random_state`. (2) Threads during fit and predict = `[ml] threads` (default 1): `n_jobs` gets that value if present, and `OMP_NUM_THREADS`, `OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS` and `MKL_NUM_THREADS` are pinned too; when not 1, the manifest's `nondeterministic_threads` and E44. (3) Sample order = (decision_ts, `instrument_id`). (4) Known nondeterministic paths such as GPUs are only marked (E44).
- **Fit id and reuse**: `fit_id` = the first 16 characters of sha256(canonical JSON{the feature node hashes, the label definition hash, the model specification (class path, normalised `get_params()`, library names and versions), the training samples (first and last decision, count, hash of the sample keys), the seed rule, the snapshot semantic hash}). Within one run, the same `fit_id` is not fitted again (overlapping walk-forward windows, the same training combination in CPCV). It is not reused between runs (since model objects are not saved).
- **Records (pickle forbidden, 2.6)**: `predictions.parquet` (decision_id, instrument_id, node, value, fit_id), `fits.parquet` (fit_id, node, tau, train_first, train_last, n_samples, n_purged, n_embargoed, model_class, params_json, lib_versions, seed, threads, fit_seconds, pred_sha256), the manifest's `ml` field. No model files or joblib.
- **rerun**: fits again and compares `pred_sha256` per fit. With threads = 1 they must be equal (E15 "prediction hash differs for fit {id}" when different); otherwise a comparison with relative tolerance 1e-9 + a notice. When a library version differs, `fit_id` differs and the result is "no fit", so the E15 sentence gets "lib {name} {v1} ≠ {v2}; rerun needs the locked environment" appended [R4F-22].
- **Cost**: fit time is outside the chapter 7 budgets (reported on a separate line). When the number of fits in a run exceeds `[validate] max_fits`, E40 before the run (does not block).
- MDA feature importance (`ml.importance`) is v1.x (reserved); clustered MDA and sample-uniqueness weighting come after that.

#### 5.3.12 Macro and event terms and the event schedule [R4F-EV-01~05, user decision 2026-10-28]
- **Timing** (INV-01): macro values and events are visible only when the availability time (`series_observations.ts_available`, `events.ts_available`, `corporate_actions.ts_announced`/`ts_terms_available`, the filing receipt time of `fundamentals_events`) ≤ `decision_ts`. Date-only publication is 5.1.4 (the next local session open). The truncation probe (6.5) runs on these terms too. The observation period (`obs_period_end`) and `ts_event` are not availability times — September CPI is visible not on September 30 but at the moment of its release on October 15 (G-96).
- **Vintage**: a revision of the same observation period is a new row (TDS 5.21, ALFRED style). `"as_of"` = the last vintage known up to the decision time, `"first"` = the first-release vintage. Neither looks at future vintages. An external series without a vintage column (`from_parquet(shape="series")`, no `vintage=`) is taken as one vintage per row, and "{name}: no vintages (revisions overwrite)" is written in the manifest `assumptions`.
- **Session axis**: a macro term is a market-level series with an as-of value for every session of the market calendar (the same axis, broadcasting and cross-section exclusion as `Market.series`; on another market's axis, 5.3.2 (6) and E17). Windows and `shift` count on the session axis, `.obs(k)` on the observation-period axis.
- **Event terms**: instrument events put values in (decision, instrument) cells: `.days_since()` = the number of sessions from the session containing the `ts_event` of that instrument's last known event to the decision session (when the event is after hours, the next session is 0), `.count(window)`, `.latest(field)`, `.occurred(window)`. An event with only `issuer_id` is put on all of that issuer's instruments at the decision time (`instruments.issuer_id`). Cells without an event are NaN (false for a Filter).
- **Event schedule `on_event`** (the 3.7 table): trigger session `s(e)` = the first schedule session with `decision_ts(s) ≥ ts_available(e)` + `delay`. The schedule is set only by event availability times and the calendar, independent of the run's start date and the account (INV-14, INV-08) — so `screen(asof=d)` knows by the same rule whether d is a trigger session and gives the same target (screen = backtest). A screen on a day that is neither a trigger session nor a lifetime-end session is `scheduled=False`; a screen on a lifetime-end session is `scheduled=True` and shows that instrument as "scheduled exit" (target 0) (the same target as backtest's exit-only decision) [R4G-03].
- **Targets of market events** [R4G-01, R4G-MG-01]: `Macro.series(...).changed()` and events with only `series_id` have no instrument. A market-level event (a macro indicator release and the like) is not tied to a particular instrument, so the engine **assumes** that its effect reaches the whole universe and makes the whole universe the decision target (`universe_scope` default `"all"`, usually the same as the schedule's full decision). If the user thinks the effect reaches only some instruments, the user narrows it with the universe or filters. This assumption is left in the manifest `assumptions` and the report header line "assumption: market-level trigger {t} affects the whole universe". Writing `universe_scope="event_instruments"` explicitly for a market event is E16 before the run, instead of silently doing nothing with an empty set.
- **Incremental decisions** (`universe_scope="event_instruments"`): the target set of trigger session d, `E_d` = the instruments triggered in that session ∩ the strategy universe. The construction, sizing and risk slots run only on `E_d`, and instruments outside `E_d` keep the previous canonical target (`TargetState`, no account) **as it is** — an event decision does not secretly shrink or grow existing targets [R4G-02]. The previous targets of instruments inside `E_d` are replaced by the new targets (taken out of the budget sum). The sizer in this mode must have `max_weight` (without it, Σ = 1 per side would eat the whole budget, so E16). Order [R4G-02, R4G-06]:
  1. **Per-side budget**: for a one-sided sleeve `1 − Σ|previous targets (outside E_d, same side)|`; for an `nx.long_short` sleeve, long `long_weight − Σ previous longs (outside E_d)`, short `short_weight − Σ|previous shorts (outside E_d)|` (0 when below 0). When a side's sum of new targets exceeds it, that side is scaled down proportionally (`targets.reason = event_budget`, counted in skips). Per-side budgets alone do not make the combined book safe (2 below).
  2. **Combined-book check**: after the per-side budgets, find the largest `k ∈ [0, 1]` for which the combined book `w(k) = w_old + k·w_new` (`w_old` = the previous targets outside `E_d`, `w_new` = the new targets of 1) keeps the sleeve's whole-book constraints — gross exposure `Σ|w| ≤ G_max`, net exposure `Σw ∈ max_net`, the feasible region — cash account `Σw⁺ + c_max·Σ|w⁻| ≤ 1` (3.7), margin account `Σ|w| ≤ min(max_gross, 1/initial_margin_max)` (5.6.7a) [R4H-01], `risk.group_caps` (per group), instrument `max_weight` — and multiply **only the new targets** by it. First, for each group cap, the new targets of that group get `k_g = clip((max − G_old,g)/G_new,g, 0, 1)`; then the closed form of the remaining linear constraints `k = min(1, (G_max − G_old)/G_new, the net-exposure boundary expression, (1 − L_old − c_max·S_old)/(L_new + c_max·S_new))`; when there is a covariance slot (`risk.vol_cap`), `σ_p(k)` is convex in k, so it is reduced further by bisection on [0, k] (tolerance 1e-9). Cut new targets leave `targets.reason = event_book_cap` and a skips row `event_book_scale` (k, k_g, the gross exposure cut `Σ|w_new|·(1−k)`), gathered in the report header line "event book cap: {n} decisions, gross cut {x}". If it still exceeds at `k = 0` (which must not happen, since the previous targets already passed the check and lifetime ends only reduce), E46. The account layer (5.5.2a) then runs on the whole combined book as it is.
  3. **Lifetime**: `hold` (sessions) — when absent, the n of the strategy's `exits.time_stop(n)`; when that is absent too, until that instrument's next event decision. Lifetime-end session `e_end` = trigger session + n sessions (the n-th session, counting the fill session as 1; a calendar count, so independent of the account). **`e_end` enters the decision grid as an exit-only decision** [R4G-03]: at that session's close decision only the event targets whose lifetime has ended are set to 0 (order intent = exit, next open), the construction, sizer and risk slots do not run, and other targets stay as they are. The same holds when there is no new event. backtest and screen follow the same rule (the screen sentence above). A lifetime of "until the next event" is replaced at the next trigger session, so it has no separate `e_end`.
  4. **When the lifetime end and `time_stop` coincide** [R4G-07]: whichever comes first exits, and only it. When an exit order and a rebalancing intent that both bring the same instrument to 0 at the same next open coincide, only the exit order is placed (the rebalancing intent is recorded in skips as `covered_by_exit`); when the lifetime end comes first, the rebalancing sale brings the holding to 0 and the sibling exit orders are cancelled as in 5.6.2a.
  Order intents are made only for `E_d`, instruments whose lifetime has ended, and cut new targets.
- **Counting and validation**: `on_event` strategies keep candidate and observation counting (6.2) and the validation methods (6.3a) as they are — the decision grid is just the collection of trigger sessions. In periods without triggers, non-event targets (canonical targets set by another schedule with `|`) stay as they are, and the event share (the targets set by event decisions) is cash once its lifetime ends [R4G-08] (report header line "event decisions: n over {k} instruments; scheduled exits: m").
- **Determinism**: several events in the same session are grouped in (`ts_available`, `event_id`) order, and events of the same instrument in the same session trigger only once.

### 5.4 Missing values and cross-sectional operations

#### 5.4.1 Missing-value rules [C7, INV-12]
- Arithmetic propagates NaN. `x/0` → NaN (+ a diagnostic count). `inf` is turned into NaN and counted.
- Filter [V31-21]: carries values together with a validity mask. A comparison with NaN → unknown. `~f`: the value is inverted and validity stays (unknown stays unknown). `&` and `|`: Kleene three-valued (false & unknown = false, true | unknown = true, the rest unknown). At the final use (`universe`, the candidates of `top`/`bottom`, `mask`, `screen`), unknown = false. So `~(Price.close.latest > 10_000)` does **not include** an instrument without a close because of a halt (v3 included it: the same result as qlib#2119). `isnan()`, `notnan()`, `isfinite()`, `isnull()` and `notnull()` are always valid. `explain` shows unknown as "unknown (input NaN: {column})". The difference from zipline (in zipline `~` turns missing into true) is in `KNOWN_DIFFERENCES.md`. **Operations mixed with unknown** [R3-13]: `all/any(window)` is Kleene (3.3), `as_factor()` turns unknown → NaN, a comparison with a Classifier's missing value (−1) is unknown, `if_else` turns unknown → NaN, and unknown in `Pipeline(screen=)` = false. The only explicit replacement is `Filter.fillna(bool)`. **Trap notice**: during a run, when the unknown ratio of the universe Filter (unknown / listed existing cells) exceeds 50% at a decision and market, `UnknownFilterNotice` (E39) is raised once with the number of decisions and the name of the leaf Filter (for example, before 2019-08-23 in the US, `~Trad.halted` is all unknown because `status_known = false` → the universe is empty; fix `(~Trad.halted).fillna(True)`). The unknown count per leaf Filter is `n_unknown_by_leaf` of `coverage.parquet`.
- The final selection (`top`, portfolio) looks only at finite scores.
- Replacement only through `fillna`, `ops.where` and `ops.coalesce`. The missing-value rules of built-in factors are in the 3.4 table.

#### 5.4.2 Definitions of cross-sectional operations (one decision, one market or the collection of `across_markets`, only cells with `mask` true and a finite value)

| Operation | Definition |
|---|---|
| `rank(method)` | 1..n (5.3.10 order). ordinal ties = the `[portfolio] tiebreak` key (default the fixed salted hash, 3.7; with `"id"`, ascending `instrument_id`) [R3-12]. average = mean rank. min/max/dense are the same as scipy `rankdata`. With `ascending=False`, the largest value is 1 |
| `zscore` | `(x − mean)/std(ddof=0)`; std = 0 → all NaN; n < 2 → NaN |
| `demean` | `x − mean` |
| `winsorize(a, b)` | `clip(x, q_a, q_b)`, q = linearly interpolated quantiles (numpy `method="linear"`) |
| `top(N)` | the first N in the order (score descending, tie key ascending) [R3-12]. When fewer than N scores are finite, all of them |
| `percentile_between(a, b)` | percentile `p = 100·(rank_average − 1)/(n − 1)` (100 when n=1); `a ≤ p ≤ b` |
| `pct_rank(higher_is_better, na)` | `p = 100·(r − 1)/(n − 1)`, r = mean rank (ascending rank when higher is better), n = number of valid cells (100 when n = 1). `na="bottom"`: missing cells are put in as "one equal value worse than the worst value", increasing n. `na="middle"`: computed on the valid cells, then missing cells = 50 [V31-25] |
| `ops.composite` | per term `pct_rank(na)` → `Σ w_k·p_k / Σ w_k` (over all terms, not only the terms valid in that cell; with `na="drop"`, a cell with any missing term is NaN) → `pct_rank` again [V31-25] |
| `groupby=g` | the definitions above per group. Cells in group −1 (unknown) are NaN/False, with the count in diagnostics |
| (no mask) | cross-section = the default domain (3.3). When a cross-sectional operation without a mask on the Strategy signal path uses a different set from the universe as its denominator, notice E34 ("rank over all listed equities, not the strategy universe; pass mask=universe") [RV-Q06] |

#### 5.4.3 Tie-preserving quantiles [11.4.5]
For each decision, with the mean rank `r` (1..n, ascending) of the n valid cells, `bucket = min(q, max(1, ceil(r·q/n)))`. Equal scores → the same mean rank → the same quantile. Quantile sizes can differ (not forced to split evenly).

### 5.5 Score → canonical target [C6, 11.4.6, INV-08, SC-01, SC-02]

#### 5.5.1 `TargetFrame`

An Arrow table, sorted by `(decision_id, selected_rank, instrument_id)`:

| Column | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| decision_id | string | |
| sleeve_id | string | |
| decision_ts | timestamp | |
| session_date | date32 | |
| instrument_id | string | |
| score | float64 | |
| selected_rank | int32 | from 1; 0 for held instruments not selected |
| stage | string | `construction` (construction) / `sizing` (sizing) / `risk` (risk, canonical final) [R4-02]. The run output `targets.parquet` has only the canonical final (`risk`) rows (with `final_weight` on those rows); with `save_signal=True`, every stage |
| pre_risk_weight | float64 | the weight output by the sizing slot (before the risk slot) [R4-02] |
| target_weight | float64 | the canonical final weight within the sleeve (market information only, INV-08 [R4-04]), **signed** (+ long, − short) [V31-LS-02]. Long only: sum ≤ 1; long–short: Σ|w| ≤ `max_gross` |
| final_weight | float64 | final target = `target_weight` × the multipliers of account rules (5.5.2a). Without account rules, bit-identical to `target_weight` [INV-23, R4-04]. The value order intents (5.6.7) use |
| side | string | `long` / `short` [V31-LS-02] |
| reason | string | selected / selected_short / capped / risk_capped / held_by_band / held_missing_score [V31-17] / conflict_long_kept [V31-LS-02] / not_selected (held but target 0) / `risk:{rule}` (reduced by a market rule) / `account:{rule}` (reduced by an account rule) [R4-04] |

- `build_targets` is a pure function that takes only the information at the decision and the target state of the previous decision (`prior`, 5.5.3) (it does not take the account state). So with the same `DecisionContext` and anchor, screen and backtest give the same table [R-13 corrected, 11.4.6, INV-08].
- The runner appends the "held but target 0" rows from the account state (they are left out when comparing canonical targets).
- The report counts, per decision, "the number of differences between the target set and the held set" (instruments not bought because of a halt or a cash shortfall and so on) (`n_target_not_held` of `coverage.parquet`).

#### 5.5.1a The sizing slot (`sizing=`) [R4-02, R4-03]

- **Place and contract**: construction (selection) → **sizing** → risk management (5.5.2) → account layer (5.5.2a) → order intent (5.6.7). Input = per sleeve and side the selection table S (id, side, score, rank) + the market window at the decision + parameters. It is a pure function that does not take the account, cash or holdings (inside INV-08; sizing from the account, like backtrader's `cash` or Qlib's `cash × risk_degree`, makes screen ≠ backtest — teardown F lesson 1). Output = per side `Σ|w| ≤ 1` (long ≥ 0; short is the same computation then multiplied by −1). Then multiplication by `long_weight` and `short_weight` of `long_short` (3.7), then the modifiers in the order written. What remains is cash. No leverage: even after the modifiers `Σ|w| ≤ min(1, max_gross)` (1 for long only) — cash account; in the margin account, only `vol_target` up to `min(max_gross, 1/initial_margin)` (5.6.7a) [R4G-MG-08].
- **Notation**: n = the number selected on the side, σ_i = `AnnualizedVolatility(L)` (L daily simple returns of `close_tr` at the decision row, ddof = 1, × √252), Σ (annual) = 252 × the sample covariance (ddof = 1) — rows = the sessions within window L where **all** the selected instruments of that side are valid (listwise deletion). Sums and products are left folds in sorted `instrument_id` order (5.3.10; no BLAS), with `wᵀΣw = Σ_i Σ_j w_i Σ_ij w_j` in lexicographic (i, j) order.
- **Instruments without input** (σ NaN or 0, no market cap, a short window) are removed from that decision's selection and counted in `skips.parquet` as (decision, id, `sizing`, reason) — they are not silently set to 0 (6.6).

| Block | Formula | Parameters (default) |
|---|---|---|
| `size.equal(max_weight=None, redistribute=False)` | `u_i = 1`, `w_i = u_i/Σu`, capped at `max_weight`, the remainder cash (with `redistribute=True`, the iterative redistribution of 3.7) | – |
| `size.value(...)` | `u_i` = market cap at the decision (3.7) | the same |
| `size.inverse_vol(lookback=252, ...)` | `u_i = 1/σ_i` | the same |
| `size.score(transform="rank", ...)` | `"rank"`: `u_i = n + 1 − rank_i` (1 = best); `"raw"`: `u_i = max(s_i, 0)` (on the short side `max(−s_i, 0)`). When all are 0, equal + skips | the same |
| `size.erc(lookback=252, min_obs=0.8, shrink=0.0)` | equal risk with risk budget `b_i = 1/n`, `Σ_δ = (1−δ)Σ + δ·diag(Σ)`, coordinate descent below | `min_obs` = a ratio of L |
| `size.vol_parity(target=0.15, lookback=252)` | `w_i = (target/n)/σ_i`; when `Σw > 1`, all multiplied by `1/Σw` (correlation ignored, the simple version of pysystemtrade) | – |
| `size.stop_risk(risk_pct=0.01, stop_pct=None, max_weight=None)` | `w_i = risk_pct/stop_pct`; without `stop_pct`, the p of the strategy's `exits.stop_loss(p)`, E16 when both are absent; after the `max_weight` cap, multiplied by `1/Σw` when `Σw > 1`. The ATR version is v1.x | – |
| modifier `size.vol_target(target, lookback=63)` (for the margin account's `G_max` and fourth term, 5.6.7a [R4H-04]) | `σ_p = √(wᵀΣw)` (the book with both sides combined, 63-day annual covariance), `k = min(target/σ_p, G_max/Σ|w|, max_weight/max|w_i|, 1/(Σw⁺ + c_max·Σ|w⁻|))` (the third term only when there is a cap; the fourth term = keeps the long–short feasible region `L + c·S ≤ 1` (3.7, 5.6.7 short collateral) after the modifier too, `c_max` = the largest `collateral_ratio` among the sleeve's markets, `1/Σw⁺` when there are no shorts) [R4F-07], `G_max = min(1, max_gross)`, `w ← k·w`. It grows weights only up to the cap (dropping the hidden leverage of bt's `TargetVol`). When `σ_p` = 0 or cannot be computed, `k = 1` + skips | `target` required |
| modifier `size.cash_floor(c)` | when `Σ|w| > 1 − c`, all multiplied by `(1 − c)/Σ|w|` | `c ∈ [0, 1)` |

- **ERC coordinate descent** [R4-03] (Griveau-Billion, Richard and Roncalli 2013, the formula of ffn's `calc_erc_weights`): start `x_i = (1/σ_i)/Σ_j(1/σ_j)`. One sweep = for each i in sorted `instrument_id` order, `σ_x = √(xᵀΣ_δx)`, `α = Σ_δ,ii`, `β = (Σ_δx)_i − x_i·α`, `γ = −b_i·σ_x`, `x_i ← (−β + √(β² − 4αγ))/(2α)` (the updated x is used immediately for the next coordinate). At the end of a sweep `Δ = Σ_i((x_i − x_i^prev)/Σx)²`; stop when `Δ < 1e-14`, at most 500 sweeps. At the end `w = x/Σx` once (no clipping; dropping the problem of LEAN RP's "clip after normalising", whose sum is not 1). **Fallback**: when (a) it does not converge within 500 sweeps, (b) `α ≤ 0`, NaN or infinite, or (c) after listwise deletion the row count < `min_obs·L`, that decision and side use the `size.inverse_vol` weights (same lookback), with an E43 `SolverFallbackNotice` count + `skips` (reason `erc_fallback:{a|b|c}`). It neither kills the run (ffn) nor hides it (LEAN). When n = 1, w = 1; when Σ is diagonal, the result is proportional to `1/σ` (G-79).
- The sizer's result is part of the canonical target, so its parameters go into `graph_hash` and `settings_hash`. A `top(weight=…)` shorthand and a strategy that states the same sizer explicitly give bit-identical targets (P-RISK-OFF (iii)).

#### 5.5.2 The risk slot (`risk=[…]`) [SC-01, OQ-2, R4-04, R4-05]

- **Order**: sizing (5.5.1a) → **the risk rule list** (market-only rules, in the order written) → exposure-cap fitting (only when shorting is on) → canonical final `target_weight` → account layer (5.5.2a: account rules, in the order written) → order intent (5.6.7). This is the same place as LEAN framework's Portfolio Construction → Risk Management. In the list, account rules must be written **after** all market rules — writing one before is E16 before the run (layer config, fix "account rules run after market rules; move drawdown_brake after vol_cap"; the order is not secretly changed — no silent failures) [R4F-26]. The report header shows the actual order "risk order: group_caps → vol_cap → [account] drawdown_brake".
- **What one rule means**: per decision it gives a per-instrument multiplier `m_i ∈ [0, 1]` (a missing id = 1), and the engine does `w_i ← m_i·w_i` (sign kept, no redistribution, the reduced share becomes cash). It does not grow a size or change a sign (longs shrink and shorts move toward 0) [V31-LS-03]. A multiplier out of range or not finite is E41 (layer your code). Changed rows get `reason = risk:{rule}`, and the per-slot effect goes into `waterfall.parquet` (5.5.2a).
- A market-only rule, like `RiskRule.apply(targets, inputs)`, takes only the table of the canonical target stage and the information at the decision (Pipeline term values, `Meta.*`, `Market.series`) and **does not take the account state** (INV-08 as it is). Account-reading rules are in 5.5.2a.
- **Exposure-cap fitting** (only when `[shorting] enabled = true`; the engine appends it after the list) [V31-LS-03, R3-07]: when shorting is off, this stage is **not called** (v3.1 always called it, and the float sum 1/9 × 9 = 1.0000000000000002 could shrink long-only weights again; INV-22). When on, `G = math.fsum(|w|)`; when `G > max_gross·(1 + 1e-12)`, every weight is multiplied by `max_gross/G`, and when `N = math.fsum(w)` is outside `max_net` (a closed interval containing 0), only the overflowing side (long or short) is shrunk proportionally to the boundary.

| Block | Reads | Formula | Parameters (default) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `risk.none()` | – | `m = 1` | – |
| `risk.group_caps(by, max, measure="gross")` | market (classification) | for group g, `G_g = Σ_{i∈g}|w_i|` (`|Σ_{i∈g} w_i|` for `"net"`); when `G_g > max`, `m = max/G_g` for i ∈ g; instruments with a missing classification are grouped in one group `"<unknown>"` and the count reported; no redistribution (the proportional reduction of LEAN's `MaximumSectorExposure`) | `by: Classifier`, `max ∈ (0, 1]` |
| `risk.regime(cond, off=0.0, on_unknown="off")` | market (a market-level Filter such as `Market.series`) | when `cond` at the decision is false (when unknown, `"off"`/`"on"` by `on_unknown`), every `m = off` | `off ∈ [0, 1]` |
| `risk.vol_cap(max_vol, lookback=63)` | market | `σ_p = √(wᵀΣw)` (the 5.5.1a definition, with the weights coming into this rule); when `σ_p > max_vol`, every `m = max_vol/σ_p` | – |
| `risk.liquidity_cap(k=0.05, lookback=20, capital="notional")` | market (account for `"nav"`) | `C` = sleeve capital: `"notional"` = `capital` × the sleeve weight, in that market's currency at the decision-time exchange rate (pure); `"nav"` = `decision_value` at the decision (becomes an account rule). `ADV_i = AverageTradedValue(lookback)`. When `|w_i|·C > k·ADV_i`, `m_i = k·ADV_i/(|w_i|·C)`; ADV is computed only when the valid sessions in the window are at least half of `lookback`, otherwise missing (a new listing from 10 sessions on) [R4F-40]; when ADV is missing, `m_i = 0` + skips | – |
| `risk.drawdown_brake(off=0.20, on=0.10, floor=0.0, mode="brake")` | **account** + shadow index (market) | 5.5.2a | – |
| `risk.portfolio_stop(loss, reset="annual")` | **account** | 5.5.2a | `reset ∈ {annual, monthly, never}` |
| `risk.no_reentry(sessions=21, after=("stop_loss","trailing_stop","time_stop"))` | **account** (exit events) | 5.5.2a | – |

- **Order can change results** (`vol_cap` computes with σ_p after the earlier rules reduced; G-86). The same order gives the same bits. The order goes into `graph_hash`.
- v1.x: correlation shock and VaR overlays (the minimum of pysystemtrade's four risk overlays), a notional cap per order. Later: risk-model-based constraints [SC-22].

#### 5.5.2a The account layer: account-reading risk rules and the final target [R4-04, R4-06, R4-09, INV-23]

- **Place**: after the canonical final `target_weight`, before order intent (5.6.7). `final_weight_i = target_weight_i × Π_k m_{k,i}` (account rules k multiplied one by one in the order written, sign kept). Without account rules this layer is **not called**, and `target_weight` is copied into `final_weight` as it is (no multiplication, INV-23). A −0.0 in the product and in the risk-slot result is rewritten as +0.0 (`0 × negative`; hash stability) [R4F-08]. A ruined (`ruined`, 5.6.7) sleeve does not call this layer (no order intents); at the ruin decision, each rule writes a last `risk_state` row `key = "suspended"`, `value = "ruined"`, and the report's rule line shows "suspended at ruin {date}" (the record does not end silently) [R4F-06].
- **Reads**: the account view up to the decision time (read-only arrays): the NAV series (daily `nav_economic`, `decision_value` for the decision session), holding quantities, `avg_cost`, first entry session, exit fill events. There are no values after the decision (INV-01).
- **State**: a rule keeps no state in its own object (dropping the rewriting of LEAN's `_portfolioHigh` and backtrader's `p.stake`). It is `(ctx, w, state_{d−1}) → (m, state_d, reason)`, and the engine writes `risk_state.parquet` (decision_id, sleeve_id, rule_index, rule, key, value_json) every decision. Checkpoints (5.6.1) and the ledger (5.9.6) hold this table, so resume = one go (INV-10) and replay hold.
- The hold band's `prior` (5.5.3) uses only canonical targets — the brake does not change the hold band's state.
- **Drawdown brake** `drawdown_brake(off, on, floor, mode)` [R4-06]:
  - NAV drawdown `DD_d = 1 − V_d/P_d`. `V_d` = the sleeve's `decision_value` at decision d (costs, interest and borrowing included), `P_d` = the maximum of daily `nav_economic` since the anchor (or the last re-entry) and `V_d`.
  - **Shadow index** S (market information and canonical targets only, not the account): `S = 1` at the anchor decision. The canonical target `target_weight` of decision d is bought at the decision session's close and held without costs until the next decision session's close (cash 0%, shorts by their sign): `S_t = S_{t−1}·(1 + Σ_i w̃_{i,t−1}·r_{i,t})`, `w̃` = the weights drifted with prices after the decision, `r` = the daily total return (`close_tr`; missing is 0 + a count). **Delisting**: when an instrument in the shadow is delisted, the last return of that instrument (including −100%) is applied once at the terminal session with the shared terminal-value function of 5.6.13 (the same function as the label `fwd_return` and accounting, R4F-04), and its weight is 0 after that (it vanishes, since cash is 0%); when the terminal value is not yet published at the decision time (after `ts_value_available`), it is left as 0 based on the last `close_tr` + a count and applied at the first decision after publication (INV-01; this prevents a missing 0 from dropping delisting losses and making recovery in a falling market look faster) [R4F-09]. `DDs_d = 1 − S_d / max_{d' ≤ d} S_{d'}`; when `S_d ≤ 0` (a shadow with short weights), `DDs_d = 1` (no re-entry), left in skips (reason `shadow_ruined`) [R4F-28].
  - `mode="brake"` (states normal/braked): in normal, when `DD_d ≥ off`, it becomes braked and **from this decision** `m = floor` (exit orders at the next session open). In braked, when `DDs_d ≤ on`, it returns to normal and from this decision `m = 1`, and at that moment `P := V_d` (the NAV peak is reset; otherwise it would trigger again at once). Why it returns by the shadow and not by NAV: while out in cash, NAV stands still and cannot show the recovery (LEAN resets the peak at once and buys again at the next rebalance, and Nautilus has no such rule — teardown F lesson 4).
  - `mode="linear"` (no state): `m = clip(1 − (DD_d − on)/(off − on), floor, 1)`.
  - E16 unless `0 < on < off < 1`. Report: triggered decisions / decisions, the brake intervals, the number of round trips, `brakes_with_shadow_ok n/m` (the number of triggers where `DDs ≤ on` already held at the triggering decision — to make visible the cases where a drawdown caused by costs, shorts or deviation led to an immediate re-entry that only paid round-trip costs; the re-entry check starts from the decision after the trigger) [R4F-28]. `risk_state` leaves `peak_source = "reentry"` at re-entry, floats in `value_json` are written in the shortest round-trip notation (`repr`), and NaN or infinity is E41 (so resume = one go holds bit for bit) [R4F-28].
- **Portfolio stop** `portfolio_stop(loss, reset)`: `V_ref` of the reference decision (`"annual"` = the first decision of each year, `"monthly"` = the first decision of each month, `"never"` = the anchor); when `V_d/V_ref − 1 ≤ −loss`, stopped, and `m = 0` until the next reference decision.
- **No re-entry** `no_reentry(sessions, after)`: after fill session s in which instrument i reached a holding of 0 through an exit rule in `after`, `m_i = 0` at decisions before market-calendar session `s + sessions` (one block for what LEAN did secretly by deleting Insights).
- **waterfall** [R4-09]: `waterfall.parquet` (decision_id, sleeve_id, step (`construction`|`sizing`|`risk:{rule}`|`exposure`|`account:{rule}`), gross_before, gross_after, net_after, n_changed, n_zeroed). The "risk waterfall" line of the report's first screen: the mean `Σ|w|` per slot and the number of triggered decisions (numerator/denominator).

#### 5.5.3 Target-state recursion and the hold band (`top(n, hold=m)`) — normative, implemented as the last ticket of v1 M6 [SC-02, OQ-1, user 2026-10-28]

v1 fixes the definitions of this section as normative (the INV-08 wording, `DecisionContext.anchor_decision_id`, the `build_targets(..., prior=)` signature) and raises E18 when `hold` is not `None`. The implementation is T7.1, the last ticket of v1's M6 (user decision 2026-10-28: included in v1). Off by default.

- **Target state** `TargetState_d = (S_long, S_short, missing_streak)` [R3-22]: `S_long` (`S_d` below) = the set of instruments with `target_weight > 0` in decision d's final canonical target, `S_short` = the set with `< 0` (per sleeve, per group when `per=` is given), `missing_streak` = for each instrument kept as `held_missing_score`, the number of consecutive decisions with a missing score. It is not the account holding (independent of fills, halts and cash). Checkpoints (5.6.1), the ledger manifest (5.9.6) and carrying over from `targets.parquet` hold all three (the sets alone cannot replay the missing-score cap).
- **Recursion**: `S_d = f(score_d, universe_d, S_{d−1})`. Candidates = instruments with a finite `signal` and a true `universe`; rank = score descending + the tie-break key of 3.7 (`tiebreak`) [V31-29]. The short side (`bottom(n, hold=m)`) runs the same recursion separately with score ascending (the mirror of the long side) [V31-LS-02].
- **Missing scores** [V31-17]: with `on_missing = "drop"` (default), an instrument of `S_{d−1}` whose score is not finite is not a candidate and drops out (as in v3; one missing value makes two round-trip trades). With `"hold"`, when `i ∈ S_{d−1}` and `universe_d(i)` is true but only the score is not finite, it stays in `K` with `reason = held_missing_score` (its rank taken as n+1), and it is removed when the number of consecutive missing decisions (`missing_streak`) exceeds `[portfolio] max_missing_decisions` (default 1). A `held_missing_score` instrument stays in K regardless of rank m (even with `hold = n`; the only cap is the consecutive missing count) [R3-22]. When that instrument's weighting input (market cap for `value`, volatility for `inverse_vol`) is also missing, the **median** of the weighting inputs `u` of the other instruments selected in that decision on the same side is used (when all are missing, that whole side is equal), left as an E30 count (v3.1's "an equal share of 1" had different units from `u` and could not be mixed) [R3-22]. It uses only the target state, so it stays a pure function (INV-08) (PyBroker "a held instrument without a bar keeps its slot", pysystemtrade "keep the previous position when the input is NaN"). (1) Keep `K = {i ∈ S_{d−1} : rank_d(i) ≤ m}` (`reason = held_by_band`, but `selected` if within rank n). (2) Add candidates not in `K` in rank order until `|S_d| = n`. Since `|S_{d−1}| ≤ n`, `|K| ≤ n`. (3) For weights, the sizing slot (5.5.1a; for the shorthand, the weighting rules of 3.7) is applied to `S_d`. The risk stage comes after that. The recursion's `prior` uses only canonical targets and not the account layer (`final_weight`, 5.5.2a) [R4-04].
- **Anchor**: the decision where the recursion starts, `S_{anchor−1} = ∅`. `backtest` = the first decision of that run (fresh capital per segment, so a new anchor per segment); `screen` = replays the recursion by running the Pipeline only on decision dates along the schedule from the `anchor` argument (default `[portfolio] anchor_date`) (30 years monthly = 360 cross-sectional computations); `live` = the decision that created the ledger (recorded in the ledger manifest).
- With the same snapshot, `screen` may carry over `S_{d−1}` from a saved `targets.parquet` (from a run with the same strategy hash and anchor) (the values are the same, so the result is the same; a response to the P2 budget).
- **INV-08 wording**: "the same strategy, snapshot, policies, anchor and `DecisionContext` give the same canonical target". With a different anchor they may not be the same, and the report writes the anchor in its header.
- **Hold-band instruments with a zero holding** [V31-17, R3-22]: the target state is not the account holding, so an instrument that was within rank n at `d−1` but could not buy a single share because of a halt, a cash shortfall or a cap, or that left through a stop loss and now has a holding of 0, stays in `K` (`held_by_band`) if it is at rank n+1..m at `d`.
