Buying this instrument anew goes against the meaning of the hold band, "sell less of what is already held", so in the execution stage (5.6.7, where the account state is used), with `[portfolio] band_entries = "skip"` (default), no opening order intent is made for an instrument with `reason = held_by_band`, a holding of 0 and no unfilled buy (on the short side, no unfilled short sale) (event `order_suppressed(band_entry)`, that share becomes cash). The target table stays as it is (INV-08). With `"buy"`, it buys as in v3. The count goes on the report's orders line.
- **Plumbing and function** [R3-22]: the serialisation, checkpoint and ledger-manifest plumbing of `TargetState` is built by M5 ticket T5.16 (always an empty state before T7.1), and T7.1 turns on the recursion function and only compares (T7.1 is still the last feature ticket of M6).
- This method meets user decision Q6 ("the hold band is a portfolio option") and removes the reason for deferral in A-10 (relying on holdings is not a pure function) [A-47].

### 5.6 Daily state machine [R-56, 11.4.2, 9.0 orders]

#### 5.6.1 Account state (per sleeve)
`AccountState` is a **column-array structure** (set in T3.3; this shape whether or not Numba is used) [RV-I10, RV-C07]:
- Money: `cash_minor: int64` (minor units of the local currency, the market rules pack's `minor_unit`; KRW 1 won, USD 1 cent). Every entry (fill amount, cost components, dividends, fractional-share cash, terminal consideration, currency exchange, borrow cost) is rounded to an integer **once**, at the moment of that entry. The rounding method is **that component's rule in the market rules pack `[rounding]`** (mode half_up / half_even / down, unit = a multiple of the minor unit; default half_up·1 = the same as v3; overridden by the user's `[rounding.<MARKET>]`), and the pack version goes into `settings_hash` [V31-05]. `down` is toward smaller size (toward 0). The value before rounding is kept in the event as a float64 `*_unrounded`.
- **Integer share arithmetic** [V31-01]: (1) A ratio is a rational number `ρ = num/den` (positive integers; TDS `ratio_num/ratio_den` is canonical). If the canonical columns are missing, 0 or negative, or only one of them is present, E13 at open (4.4-4) [R3-06, R4F-01]. **The engine does not infer fractions from f64** (user decision 2026-10-28 [R4F-01]): from f64 alone one cannot decide whether 0.333 is 1/3 or 333/1000 (Sol's counterexample: if the exact value 0.3333 = 3333/10000, 30,000 shares → 9,999 shares, but inference makes 10,000 shares), and wherever the threshold is set a counterexample remains (0.0476 → 21 shares → 0 shares, 0.124999998 → 8 shares → 0 shares). The one exception is the user's choice `[corporate_actions] decimal_ratio = "literal"`: only for events without an exact ratio, the decimal of `repr(x)` (the Python 3.13 shortest round-trip notation; in exponent notation written out with an integer mantissa and exponent) is used as is as an exact decimal fraction (0.333 → 333/1000, 1.000004 → 250001/250000), leaving the event `ratio_decimal_literal`, an E30 count and the report header line "decimal ratios used literally: n events". v3.2's three-step inference rule (rounded repeating decimal, computation noise, decimal as is; thresholds q ≤ 20·1e-8·denominator 10,000) was removed (`sources/engine-spec-v3.3-2026-10-28.md` 5.6.1). (2) `floor_shares(x)` = with `n = round(x)`, n if `|x − n| ≤ 1e-6`, otherwise `floor(x)` (so that the floating-point product `0.57·100000/1000 = 56.99999999999999` does not lose a share). `floor_lot(x) = lot·floor_shares(x/lot)`. (3) Ratio products are in integers: `q' = (|q|·num) div den`, fractional share `frac = ((|q|·num) mod den)/den`, the sign is the sign of q (short = negative) [V31-LS-05]; the intermediate product `|q|·num` is computed as a Python integer (or an overflow-checked int64) [R3-06]. Results such as LEAN#9882 (16 shares, 1:8 reverse split → 1 share), zipline-reloaded#336 and vectorbt#894, where "one share of an integer-multiple holding becomes fractional-share cash", do not occur.
- **Events without an exact ratio — removing the instrument** [R7-01, user decision 2026-10-29]: when `decimal_ratio = "exclude_instrument"` (default), an instrument i whose share-changing event e (split, consolidation, bonus issue, stock dividend, capital reduction, rights issue, spin-off) lacks `ratio_num/ratio_den` or is marked unverified by the producer is handled as follows. (1) **Visible time** v(e) = the public time (`ts_announced` → `ts_terms_available` → if neither exists, `ts_effective`; date only, 5.1.4). (2) From the first decision with `decision_ts ≥ v(e)`, i **leaves the universe** of that sleeve (the same rule for screens, evaluation and strategies, screen = backtest). It is not removed before v(e) — filtering in advance by a future event would be future information. (3) If i is held, the target of that decision becomes 0 and it is sold by the ordinary fill rules (bought back if short). (4) If that order cannot fill before `ts_effective` (the ex-date or consolidation effective date), it is treated as closed at the last valid close of the session just before, paying costs (reason `ca_unverified_exit`, manifest `assumptions`). It is not valued at prices after the event. (5) Feature values of that instrument whose window crosses event e are NaN (the adjustment factor is unknown; INV-12, no filling). Features that use only the interval before the event are used as they are. (6) Even if no later event with an exact ratio follows event e, i does not come back until the end of the run (v1, a simple rule). (7) The report header line "{n} instruments excluded for missing exact ratios ({m} events; first {date})", an E30 warning, `skips` (reason `ca_ratio_missing`), and the list of instruments and events in the manifest `policies.corporate_actions`. `require_exact` gives E13 in this case, and `literal` is as in the table above.
- **Signed holdings** [V31-LS-04]: `qty[int64]` is positive for longs and negative for shorts. A short holding's `avg_cost` is the average short-sale price, and in the place of `high_water_close` a short uses the lowest close since entry (`low_water_close`, updated by `min`, 5.6.3(6)) [R3-10]. When shorting is off, `qty ≥ 0` is asserted.
- Holdings (instrument integer index i = the sort position of `instrument_id` in that market block, column length = the number of instruments in the block): `qty[int64]`, `sellable_qty[int64]`, `locked_until_session[int32]` (lock on new stock-dividend shares), `avg_cost[float64]` (raw price, not an entry), `high_water_close[float64]`, `opened_session[int32]`.
- Order table (5.6.2): row = order, columns = `order_seq[int64]`, `instrument_idx[int32]`, `purpose[int8]`, `side[int8]`, `type[int8]`, `at[int8]`, `tif[int8]`, `limit_price[f64]`, `stop_price[f64]`, `max_age_sessions[int32]` (−1 = none), `requested_qty`, `filled_qty`, `cancelled_qty`, `remaining_qty` (int64), `trigger_state[int8]`, `status[int8]`, `oco_group[int64]`, `eligible_from_session[int32]`, `age_sessions[int32]`, `decision_price[f64]`, `decision_id_idx[int32]`. The strings `order_id` and `decision_id` are in a side dictionary table.
- Receivables table: `kind`, `instrument_idx`, `amount_minor[int64]`, `pay_session[int32]`, `estimated[bool]`, `ts_known[int64 us]`. Table of entitlements of unknown amount: `event_chain_id`, `instrument_idx`, `qty[int64]`, `ts_terms_expected`, the after-the-fact amount (for reports, not a decision input).
- `seq` (event sequence number), `state_version` (ledger transaction, 5.9.6), `target_state` (`TargetState = (S_long, S_short, missing_streak)`, 5.5.3; always empty before T7.1) [R3-22], the sleeve state `ruined[bool]`·`ruined_session[int32]` [R3-09], short collateral `reserved_minor[int64]` (5.6.7), the ledger manual-instruction table (5.9.6) [V31-18, V31-LS-04].
- **Seam** [R3-26]: this state and the entry functions sit behind the Arrow-array seam (2.4) of `nx.accounting._state`.
- **Checkpoint** = Arrow IPC (uncompressed) of the arrays above + a meta JSON (`schema = "nexusbt-checkpoint/1"`, the last processed session and phase, the snapshot ids, `state_version`). No pickle (2.6). The same state + the same input give the same result [INV-10].

#### 5.6.2 Orders and status

`Order` (one row of the order table): `order_id` (= sha256(decision_id, instrument_id, purpose, seq)[:16]), `decision_id`, `sleeve_id`, `instrument_id`, `purpose` (rebalance / exit:<rule>), `side` (buy / sell / sell_short / buy_to_cover [V31-LS-06]; one order does not cross the holding's sign — an intent to go from long 10 shares → short 5 shares is two orders, `sell 10` + `sell_short 5`, and the opening one fills only after the reducing one is done, 5.6.7 [R3-10]), `type` (market / limit / stop / stop_limit), `at` (open/close; market only), `limit_price`, `stop_price`, `tif` (day/gtc), `max_age_sessions` (`int | None`; always None for exit orders), the four quantities `requested_qty`, `filled_qty`, `cancelled_qty`, `remaining_qty` (INV-18: `requested = filled + cancelled + remaining`), `trigger_state` (`untriggered` / `triggered`; stop and stop_limit only), `oco_group` (exit orders), `ts_created`, `eligible_from_session`, `age_sessions`, `status`, `decision_price` (the raw close of the decision row `row_m(d)`; for an exit order, the raw close of the session that created it) [SC-11, RV-C05].

Three things are kept apart: the **life status** (`status`, the table below), the **trigger state** (`trigger_state`) and the **quantity accounting** (the four quantities). A partial cancellation (cash shortfall, split fractional shares) is not a status transition but a quantity event (`qty_cancelled(reason, qty)`), which increases `cancelled_qty` and decreases `remaining_qty`. When `remaining_qty = 0`, the order goes to an end status: `FILLED` if `filled_qty = requested_qty`, otherwise `CANCELLED` (the last reducing reason).

| Status | Entry condition |
|---|---|
| `NEW` | created at a decision (before the fill session) |
| `ACCEPTED` | the start of `eligible_from_session` |
| `TRIGGERED` | the stop condition of a stop or stop_limit is met. From then on **the trigger persists** (even if the price comes back after a partial fill, a cap or a block, it does not return to `untriggered`): a stop's remaining quantity is a market order at the next phase or session (the next open phase, price O), a stop_limit is like a limit order at Lp [RV-C05] |
| `PARTIALLY_FILLED` | partly filled, remaining quantity > 0 |
| `FILLED` | remaining quantity 0 (end) |
| `CANCELLED` | replaced by a new decision (`replaced`), sleeve ruin (`ruined`, rebalance orders only [R3-09]), holding gone (`position_closed`), the OCO counterpart brought the holding to 0 (`oco`), delisting (`delisted`), the whole remaining quantity reduced to 0 by quantity events (`insufficient_cash` etc.) (end; `filled_qty` is recorded as it is) |
| `EXPIRED` | end of session for a day order, a **rebalance** GTC reaching `max_age_sessions` (default 20). `purpose = exit:*` orders do not expire (P-STATE forbids the exit → EXPIRED transition) (end) |
| `REJECTED` | quantity 0 after lot rounding, the instrument does not exist in the execution session (end) |

Allowed transitions: NEW→ACCEPTED|CANCELLED|REJECTED, ACCEPTED→TRIGGERED|PARTIALLY_FILLED|FILLED|CANCELLED|EXPIRED, TRIGGERED→PARTIALLY_FILLED|FILLED|CANCELLED|EXPIRED, PARTIALLY_FILLED→PARTIALLY_FILLED|FILLED|CANCELLED|EXPIRED. PARTIALLY_FILLED of a triggered order keeps `trigger_state = triggered`. Any other transition and any INV-18 violation is an `AssertionError` (engine bug). Apart from status, the per-session event `blocked(reason)` (halted / limit_locked / zero_volume / no_open / no_range / capacity_unknown / tradability_unknown_blocked / short_ban [V31-LS-07] / collateral [V31-LS-04] / override [V31-18] / closing_leg_pending [R3-10]) is recorded. The part of a cover beyond the short size is the quantity event `qty_cancelled(over_cover)` [R3-10]. When no order intent is made at all, it is the decision event `order_suppressed(reason)` (small / band / band_entry / override / ruined [R3-09]) [V31-06, V31-17, V31-18]; when a price was snapped to the grid, the order event `price_rounded` (original value `*_unrounded`) [V31-05]. `no_open` = that row's `open` is null, `no_range` = `high` or `low` is null (4.5 F-01).

#### 5.6.2a Life of an exit order [RV-C05, R-56]

1. **Creation**: **right after** the fill event of an entry (a fill that takes the holding from 0 to non-zero — a long buy or a short sale [R3-10]), **within the same phase**, the holding's exit orders (`Strategy(exits=)`) are created. `ts_created` = that fill instant, quantity = the holding's sellable quantity at that instant (for a short, the short size; under the immediate-settlement research model, quantity bought in the same session is sellable too; shares locked from a stock dividend are excluded), price basis = the `avg_cost` at that instant (a trailing stop uses `high_water_close` starting from the fill price, a short `low_water_close`). `eligible_from_session` = that session.
2. **Same-bar participation**: exit orders created after an open-phase entry take part in the intraday phase of the same bar (G-08). Exit orders created after an intraday-phase entry take **only the adverse side** in that bar (long stop loss: fills if `L ≤ Sp`, short stop loss (cover stop): fills if `H ≥ Sp` [R3-10]; a take-profit does not fill in that bar, 5.6.4). Exit orders created after a close-phase entry start from the next session.
3. **Resizing**: at every event that changes the holding (an additional buy fill, a partial sell fill, a split or consolidation, the unlock of new stock-dividend shares), the `requested`/`remaining` of all the holding's exit orders are immediately set to the new sellable quantity (quantity event `exit_resized`, keeping INV-18: an increase raises `requested`, a decrease goes to `cancelled_qty`), and the price basis is recomputed from the new `avg_cost`. A split follows the price ÷ρ rule of 5.6.10.
4. **OCO**: when one exit order fills partly, its sibling orders are **not cancelled** but reduced to the remaining holding (the resizing of 3). When the holding reaches 0, all remaining exit orders are `CANCELLED(position_closed)` (reason `oco` when it ended through OCO). Sibling orders are cancelled only when the remaining holding is 0 (the same for the same-bar rule of 5.6.4) [R3-10].
5. **No expiry**: `max_age_sessions = None`, `tif` is `gtc`. Even when a new decision replaces the rebalance orders, exit orders stay (resizing only).
6. Tests: G-08 (same-bar stop loss after an open entry), G-37 (a partly filled stop loss stays triggered even when the price recovers the next day), G-38 (the remaining holding is protected after a partial OCO), G-39 (a stop loss is still valid after 25 sessions), P-STATE (exit → EXPIRED forbidden), P-QTY.

#### 5.6.3 Session processing order [11.4.2, RV-C02]
The events of all sleeves are processed by a UTC-ordered queue. At the same UTC instant, the **phase order** is `session_start < open < intraday < close_fill < close_mark < decision < session_end`, then sleeve name order (v2's "open < decision < close" contradicted the processing phases and was removed). So a decision always sees the same-instant close fills and valuations up to that instant (at `KR_close`, a KR decision reflects that day's KR close fill result `q_i`, G-50). In session t of market m:

1. **`session_start`** (just before `ts_open(t)`): (0) **Accrual** [R3-09, R3-25]: with the short quantity q at the end of the previous session t−1 and its `close_mark` valuation price, book the borrow cost `|q|·mark·borrow_bps/1e4 × (date_t − date_{t−1})/day_count` (event `borrow_fee`, leg `financing`) and the rebate `|q|·mark·short_rebate_bps/1e4 × the same days` (event `short_rebate`), and on `cash_minor > 0` at the end of the previous session the cash interest `cash·cash_rate_bps/1e4 × the same days` (event `cash_interest`) (`day_count` is a pack key, seed 365; date = the calendar-day difference of the market calendar's `session_date`). Only nights actually held are charged: a short at the end of Friday pays 3 days at the start of Monday, a short opened on Monday pays 1 day at the start of Tuesday, a short opened intraday and closed the same day pays 0, and on a day covered at the open the previous night's share is still paid. The first session of the run has no accrual. It is a mandatory obligation, so it is booked even when cash is short (5.6.7). (a) The effective corporate actions (5.6.10–5.6.12) of the resolver (5.3.3a, reference time = this instant) — quantity, order and average-price adjustment (`order_rebased` event), entitlement creation, exit-order resizing; (b) entitlements whose amount is public by this instant → receivables (correction entries included); (c) receivables whose pay date has come → cash; (d) terminal events (5.6.13): in the terminal session, order cancellation, holding removal, terminal entitlement creation. The order (0)→(d) is fixed.
2. **`open`**: among orders with `eligible_from_session <= t`, those subject to open fills (market `at=open`, limit, stop and stop_limit orders whose condition the open satisfies, the remaining quantity of triggered stops). **Risk-reducing orders first**: long sells → short covers (`buy_to_cover`) → short sales (`sell_short`) → long buys [V31-LS-06] — this order of the four groups and the `buying_power` allocation (5.6.7) are the same in all three fill phases (open, intraday, close_fill) [R3-10] (with shorting off it is the same as v3's "sells first, then buys"; 5.6.7 cash allocation; open sell proceeds may be used for buys at the same open — the same auction instant, the immediate-settlement research model). Within the same group, (purpose: exit first, rebalance next) → `instrument_id` order. Exit orders are created at every entry fill (5.6.2a).
3. **`intraday`**: the remaining limit, stop and stop_limit orders by the H/L rules (5.6.4). The order of the four groups (long sells → covers → short sales → long buys), within the same group (exit first) → `instrument_id` order [R3-10]. **Intraday sell proceeds are not used for buys in this phase** (the fill instant is unknown, so an afternoon sell cannot pay for a morning buy; `intraday_proceeds = next_phase`, recorded in the manifest under the daily-bar approximation name) [RV-C06]. Intraday increasing orders (short sales, long buys) stay only within the `buying_power` at the start of this phase — the proceeds and collateral releases of reducing fills in the same phase are not used [R3-10]. Exit orders created after an intraday entry fill go to step 3b, which judges only the adverse side in the same bar (5.6.2a). OCO conflicts go the adverse way (5.6.4).
4. **`close_fill`**: market `at=close` orders at the close (order of the four groups [R3-10]). Intraday sell proceeds can be used from here on.
5. For every fill, costs are computed once and booked in integer minor units (5.6.8) [11.4.2].
6. **`close_mark`**: holding × raw close. Without a close, the **stale valuation price** = the last valid raw close (session s0) × Π (the price factors `m_e` of events that took effect from after s0 up to this session and that the resolver knows at this time, `C[t]/C[s0]` of 5.3.3), `mark_status = stale_adjusted` (`stale` when there is no event) [V31-02]. So a 2:1 split during a halt does not double the NAV (20 shares × 50, G-54), and an ex-dividend date during a halt lowers the price by the price factor, not counting it twice with the receivable (LEAN#9819 MACK case, bt#570). The same value is used by `decision_value`'s `mark` (5.6.9), the target share count's `P_i` (5.6.7) and the terminal entitlement's basis (5.6.13). A short holding multiplies the same valuation price by a negative quantity. Borrow cost, rebates and cash interest accrue from this valuation at the start of the next session ((0) of (1); v3.1's "this session's closing quantity × days since the previous valuation" charged nights not held and missed nights held) [V31-LS-04, R3-09]. Trailing basis update — long `high_water_close = max(high_water_close, close)`, short `low_water_close = min(low_water_close, close)` [R3-10] — and exit-price recomputation, the ruin check (5.6.7) [R3-09], then **expiry**: the remaining part of day orders → EXPIRED. Rebalance GTC orders `age_sessions += 1`, reaching `max_age_sessions` → EXPIRED (exit orders excluded).
7. **`decision`**: if this instant is this sleeve's `decision_ts`, signal → canonical target → order intents (5.6.7; sizing inputs are only values knowable at `decision_ts`, 5.6.9; a `ruined` sleeve makes no order intents [R3-09]). Remaining rebalance orders are `CANCELLED(replaced)`; exit orders stay.
8. **`session_end`**: state recording (a candidate point for a checkpoint).

#### 5.6.4 Daily-bar fill rules [9.0 orders, R-56]
Notation: that session's raw prices `O, H, L, C`. Limit price `Lp`, stop price `Sp`. `through` = for a limit fill the price must pass through the limit (`[execution] limit_fill`, default), `touch` = touching is enough. Principle: inside one bar, all that is known is that O is first and C is last; the order of H and L is unknown. **An unknown order is assumed to be the one adverse to the order owner.** Inside a bar the price is taken to move without breaks (no intraday gaps).

| Order | Open phase | Intraday phase (when not filled at the open) |
|---|---|---|
| Market buy/sell `at=open` | price O | – |
| Market `at=close` | – | price C in the close phase |
| Limit buy Lp | O ≤ Lp → price O | through: L < Lp, touch: L ≤ Lp → price Lp |
| Limit sell Lp | O ≥ Lp → price O | through: H > Lp, touch: H ≥ Lp → price Lp |
| Stop sell Sp | O ≤ Sp → price O (gap) | L ≤ Sp → price Sp |
| Stop buy Sp | O ≥ Sp → price O | H ≥ Sp → price Sp |
| Stop-limit sell (Sp, Lp ≤ Sp) | O ≤ Sp: O ≥ Lp → price O; O < Lp → TRIGGERED, the limit sell Lp rule intraday | L ≤ Sp → TRIGGERED, price Sp (continuous in-bar path assumed: the price at the trigger instant = Sp ≥ Lp, so it can fill at once; the same for `Lp = Sp`). The part left by a cap or a block carries over as TRIGGERED |
| Stop-limit buy (Sp, Lp ≥ Sp) | O ≥ Sp: O ≤ Lp → price O; O > Lp → TRIGGERED, the intraday limit buy Lp rule | H ≥ Sp → TRIGGERED, price Sp (the same boundary rule) |
| Triggered stop (remaining quantity, next session) | price O, like a market order | – |
| Triggered stop_limit (remaining quantity, next session) | the limit Lp rule | the limit Lp rule |

- **Both touched in the same bar**: when the OCO exit orders of one holding (for example stop loss Sp and take-profit Tp) both meet their condition in the intraday phase, only the stop loss (the adverse side) fills. The other one is `CANCELLED(oco)` if the stop loss brings the remaining holding to 0, and if only part fills because of a cap or a block, it is reduced to the remaining holding and kept (5.6.2a(4)) [R3-10]. When it is decided in the open phase (O ≤ Sp or O ≥ Tp), the open side comes first.
- **Exit after a same-bar entry**: 5.6.2a(2). For an entry filled at the open, exit orders are valid in that bar's intraday phase. For an entry filled intraday (limit or stop), **only the adverse-side exit** is valid in that bar: a stop loss fills if L ≤ Sp, a take-profit does not fill in that bar.
- **Trigger persists**: for a triggered stop or stop_limit, the part not filled that day stays triggered in the next session too (stop → market at the next open, stop_limit → limit Lp; rebalance orders follow their TIF and `max_age_sessions`, exit orders do not expire) [RV-C05].
- **Assumption sentence**: in the manifest `assumptions`, "stops fill at the stop price unless the open gaps through it; continuous intraday path assumed (no intraday jumps)". A stricter model that allows intraday gaps (`stop_limit_fill = close_confirmed`) is an optional knob for v1.x [RV 2.4].
- For fills of limit and stop orders judged on daily bars, `FillWarning` (error E11, a warning) is recorded once in the run warnings: "daily-bar fills for limit/stop orders are approximations; intraday sequencing requires minute/tick data (E4)" [research 2.5].
- When minute or tick data exist (E4), the intraday sequence is judged with that data (chapter 11). The v1 daily-bar rules remain then as the daily-bar mode.
- **Fill record** [SC-11]: for every fill, `fill_resolution` (v1 = `"1d"`; later `"1m"`, `"tick"`), `fill_rule` (`open`, `gap_open` (the open passed the stop price, filled at the open), `limit_open`, `intraday_adverse` (intraday H/L judgement), `close`, `zipline_close` (preset)) and `arrival_price` (the raw open O of the execution session; null if absent) are recorded. A run with mixed judgement resolutions shows "x/n fills judged on daily bars" in the report (a quantification of E11). With `decision_price` and `arrival_price`, implementation shortfall (fill price vs decision price) can be computed after the fact (later TCA).
- If `O` is null, orders due in the open phase are `blocked(no_open)`; if `H` or `L` is null, orders due in the intraday phase are `blocked(no_range)` (0 filled, carried over). If `C` is null with `obs_status = traded`, it is E14 (`TDS-V004`) and never reaches here.
- **Mirror for shorts** [V31-LS-06]: a short sale (`sell_short`) uses the "sell" rows of the table as they are, and a cover (`buy_to_cover`) the "buy" rows (limit short sale Lp: O ≥ Lp → O, intraday H > Lp → Lp; cover stop Sp: O ≥ Sp → O (gap), intraday H ≥ Sp → Sp). In the OCO of one short holding, when the stop loss (above) and the take-profit (below) are both touched in the same bar, only the stop loss (the adverse side). A short stop loss after a same-bar entry is `H ≥ Sp` (the adverse side) [R3-10]. Costs (½ spread, impact) use the same formula regardless of side.

#### 5.6.4a Price grid [V31-05]
- **Grid source** (the first that exists, from the front): instrument `instruments.tick_size` → the `tick_band` of TDS `market_rules` (instrument `tick_rule_id` or market·`venue_group`, valid on that date) → the rules pack `[grid] tick_table` → the currency minor unit (F-15). In a price-band table, "the band of that price" is decided by the value before snapping.
- **Snapping order prices**: when an order is made (decision phase, exit-order creation, resizing), the computed Lp and Sp are snapped to the grid in the **conservative direction** — buy limits and cover limits down, sell and short-sale limits up, long sell stops down, short cover stops up, and for a stop-limit each of the two prices. The original value is `price_unrounded`, event `price_rounded`. They are snapped again after a split rebase (`price/ρ`) and a trailing recomputation. If the snapped price is 0 or below, the order is `REJECTED`. Snapping may cross a price-band boundary: when the original value is near a boundary, it goes to the nearest valid grid price in that direction (KR buy 4,997.5 → 4,995, sell 4,997.5 → 5,000). When the rule date changes and the price of a pending GTC order is not on the new grid, it is snapped again in the same direction at `session_start` that day, leaving the event `order_rebased(grid)` [R3-02].
- **Judgement in integers of the market minimum unit** [R3-02]: every price comparison (limit and stop conditions, near the upper and lower limits, same-bar judgement) is made in integers on one coordinate `units(P) = round(P / base_unit)` (pack `[grid] base_unit`, KR 1 won, US 0.0001 dollars; `|P/base_unit − units| ≤ 1e-9·|P/base_unit|`). v3.1's "the tick count of that price `round(P/tick)`" flipped the order when the two operands were in different price bands, because the denominators differed (4,995/5 = 999 > 5,000/10 = 500). The tick table is used only for checking grid membership and snapping order prices. The bar data's `0.6768000000000001` is 6768 units (US; freqtrade#13661: an exit at the end vanished over a 1-ULP difference). An observed bar price that does not fit a minimum-unit integer within 1e-9 (data wrongly declared as adjusted prices, etc.) is **not moved**; only that bar's comparisons are made in floating point, and an `off_grid_bar` count (market·year, numerator/denominator) is recorded [R3-14]. The fill price is the snapped order price (Lp, Sp) or the data's O or C as is.
- When the price limits `U` and `D` (5.6.5) are made as computed values, they are snapped to the grid too (upper limit down, lower limit up — the conservative direction that widens the blocked side; the data's `up/down_limit_price` as is).
- Assumption sentence: "order prices snapped conservatively to the market price grid ({source})". The zipline preset turns grid snapping off (5.6.15).

#### 5.6.5 Tradability [11.4.2, design 3.6, C4]
Cases where an order cannot fill in that session (0 filled, the order remains and carries over, event `blocked`):

| Condition | What it blocks |
|---|---|
| `bars.obs_status ≠ traded` (halted / missing / no_trade) | everything |
| `tradability_daily.halted_full_day = true` | everything |
| (markets with price limits, rules pack `price_limits`) open near the upper limit: `O ≥ U·(1 − tol)` | open-phase buys |
| (the same) open near the lower limit: `O ≤ D·(1 + tol)` | open-phase sells |
| `locked_up = true` | intraday and close buys |
| `locked_down = true` | intraday and close sells |
| `status_known = false` and `unknown_tradability = "block"` | everything |
| a ban interval of the rules pack `[shorting] ban_table` (half-open, `scope` grammar, union of `except_universe_ids` — 4.7) [R3-15] | new short sales (`sell_short`) only; covers and long trades allowed [V31-LS-07] |
| ledger manual instruction `no_trade` / `reduce_only` (the increasing side) / `close` (only the reducing side allowed) | as instructed [V31-18] |

- `U`, `D` = `tradability_daily.up_limit_price/down_limit_price`. If absent, `U = ref·(1 + pct)`, `D = ref·(1 − pct)`, `ref` = `tradability_daily.reference_price` (if absent, the previous raw close), `pct` is looked up in the order `tradability_daily.price_limit_ratio` → the `price_limit` of TDS `market_rules` (v1.2, if present) → that market's rules pack `price_limits.table` (KR pack = `kr_price_limits_v1.csv`), and the computed U and D are snapped to the price grid (5.6.4a) [V31-05] (the session table has no limit width, TDS-D8). A package without `tradability_daily` blocks only by `obs_status`, without halt or limit rules, and E30. `tol = [execution] kr_limit_tolerance` (default = the rules pack's `open_tolerance`, KR 0.005; a conservative margin that covers tick rounding and approximation; the key name is kept for v1 compatibility) [A-13, RV-I08].
- Whether an open fill is possible is not judged from the close's upper/lower limit mark (`limit_up_close`) [C4].
- `status_known = false` (US before 2019-08-23) is `assume_tradable` by default, and the number of such fills is reported as `tradability_unknown_fills` [11.4.2].

#### 5.6.6 Volume cap and partial fills [11.4.2, C4]
- `ADV_t` = the mean of the t-based adjusted volume over the sessions among t−20..t−1 with `obs_status = traded` (`adv_window` 20). If the observations are fewer than `adv_min_obs` (10), it is unknown → 0 filled in that session, event `blocked(capacity_unknown)`.
- Cap `q_max,t = floor_lot(min(η · ADV_t, ψ · volume_t))`, `η = max_participation` (default 0.05), `ψ = max_bar_participation` (default 0.10), `volume_t` = that session's actual raw volume (in t-based adjusted units) [V31-03]. The first term is **an approximation of past-day liquidity, not a measurement of open liquidity** (manifest assumption wording). The second term is a physical cap, "no more can fill than the whole market's volume that day": it stops impossible fills in instruments whose usual ADV is large but which traded very little that day (right after a halt is lifted, thinly traded micro caps — included by default) (Qlib `_clip_amount_by_volume`, the zipline preset's same-bar 10%). That day's volume is **not a decision input but an after-the-fact feasibility cap on fill quantity** (INV-01): it does not put future information into decisions, but it is an approximation that can move results in either direction (it can also avoid bad fills by shrinking fills on low-volume days), and open fills use the whole day's volume too (not the opening single-price auction volume). The manifest assumption sentence "fill quantity capped by realized session volume (ex-post bound); full-session volume used for open fills", and in the report's orders line the number of fills bound by ψ / total [R3-03]. The binding reason is the fill event's `capacity_bound_reason ∈ {adv, bar_volume}`, and the capacity scenarios (5.7) follow the same rule. Hand calculation G-56 (ADV 1,000,000 · that day's volume 2,000 · order 50,000 → 200 shares filled, 49,800 carried over).
- The cap is shared by all orders per (sleeve, instrument, **physical direction**, session) — buy direction = long buys + covers, sell direction = long sells + short sales [R3-10] (each on the same ADV basis; the same in the ×10 and ×100 of the capacity scenarios). Orders that come first in the processing order (5.6.3) use it first.
- The part left over because of the cap stays on the order and carries over to the next session (PARTIALLY_FILLED).

#### 5.6.7 Order quantities and cash [11.4.2]
- **Ruin check** [R3-09, user decision 2026-10-28]: at the moment the `decision_value` (5.6.9) of a sleeve with shorts becomes 0 or below (after `close_mark` or just before a decision, whichever comes first), that sleeve becomes `ruined`, and this is not undone. After that the sleeve makes no new order intents (the decision phase is skipped, `order_suppressed(ruined)`), remaining rebalance orders are `CANCELLED(ruined)`, and exit orders (which only reduce holdings) stay and fill by the "Exit orders and cash" rule below [R4F-06]. Mandatory obligations (borrow cost, short dividends, merger cash, terminal obligations, tax payments) keep being booked (negative cash, below). The check comes **before** computing `T_i`, so `V ≤ 0` does not flip the sign of targets. Other sleeves keep running (the combined NAV is the sum). The report header line "ruined {sleeve} on {date}", `ruined` and `warnings` in the ledger's `status.json`, an E38 summary; metrics that assume positive wealth (CAGR, Sharpe, Sortino, MDD, PSR, DSR) are "n/a (ruin)" for that sleeve and the combined series; the NAV series is not cut (a short book can lose more than its starting capital). The cash account (default) does not simulate forced liquidation (the margin account has the margin-call reduction and full liquidation at negative equity of 5.6.7a [R4H-02, R6-07]).
- **Quantities fixed at the decision**: the sleeve decision value `V = decision_value` (5.6.9; only values knowable at the decision). Executable weight `w'_i = w_i·(1 − cost_reserve_bps/1e4)` (`w_i` = the final target `final_weight`, 5.5.2a; without account rules, bit-identical to the canonical target [R4-04]) (default reserve 0, recorded in the manifest) [RV-Q07]. Target share count `T_i = sign(w_i)·floor_lot(|w'_i|·V / P_i)` (integer arithmetic 5.6.1 [V31-01]; signed [V31-LS-02]), `P_i` = the raw close of `row_m(d)` (if absent, the stale valuation price of 5.6.3 [V31-02]). Order quantity `Δ_i = T_i − q_i` (`T_i = 0` if the holding is not in the target). `Δ_i > 0` buys (covers if short), `< 0` sells (the part where a long goes below 0 is a short sale), 0 means no order. A Δ that crosses the holding's sign is split into two orders (reduce + open the other side), sent together at the same decision [V31-LS-06]. The opening one (short sale or long buy) fills only after the holding has become 0 at the fill instant (short sale `q ≤ 0`, long buy `q ≥ 0`); otherwise it carries over in that phase as `blocked(closing_leg_pending)` (when the reducing one filled only partly because of a cap, a halt or a lock) [R3-10].
- **Filtering small orders** [V31-06]: with values at the decision, (a) if `|Δ_i|·P_i < max(min_trade_value, min_trade_weight·V)`, that order intent is not made (`order_suppressed(small)`); (b) with `rebalance_band = b`, for an instrument that is held and is in the target with the same sign, if `|w_held − w_target| ≤ b·|w_target|` it is not made (`order_suppressed(band)`), and outside it `band_mode = "edge"` (default) trades only up to the band edge `w_target·(1 ± b)` (the nearer side), while `"target"` trades up to the target (pysystemtrade `buffer_trade_to_edge`). **Neither applies to new entries, full exits (target 0) or sign changes.** The target table is unchanged, so INV-08 holds. By default all are off (the same results as v3), and the knobs go into `settings_hash`. In the report's orders line, "suppressed: small {m}, band {n}" (even when off, "orders below 0.1% of sleeve: n (cost x)" is shown). The zipline preset turns them off.
- **Hold-band entry and manual instructions**: `band_entries = "skip"` (5.5.3) [V31-17] and ledger manual instructions (5.9.6) [V31-18] cut order intents here (event `order_suppressed`).
- **Recording the target gap** [V31-14]: at every decision, `rounding_gap = Σ|w'_i| − Σ|T_i|·P_i/V` (intended executable gross allocation − gross allocation after rounding; long–short included, intended cash and cap leftovers are not counted as rounding — v3.1's `rounding_cash_weight = 1 − Σ…` had the wrong meaning for under-invested long books and long–short books [R3-20]), `n_zero_lot_targets` (target > 0 but `T_i = 0`) and the weight gap after rounding `½Σ|w_i − T_i·P_i/V|` are recorded in `coverage.parquet`. If `rounding_gap > 0.05`, `LotRoundingWarning` (E37, number of decisions, median). While waiting, it is not re-matched every day. Splits adjust the quantity [11.4.2].
- **Sellable quantity**: a long sell order's fill does not exceed `sellable_qty` (new stock-dividend shares are locked before the pay date). Short sales do not depend on holdings (no borrowable-quantity data, assumption sentence for the pack's `borrow_availability = "assume_all"`) [V31-LS-07]. A cover fill does not exceed the short size `|q|` (the excess is `qty_cancelled(over_cover)`; no long is quietly opened on the other side) [R3-10].
- **Short collateral** [V31-LS-04]: `reserved = Σ_{shorts} |q_i|·mark_i·(1 + collateral_ratio[m])` (valuation point: open phase = that session's O, intraday phase = the value at the start of the phase, close phase = C [R3-10]). Usable cash `buying_power = cash − reserved`. A short-sale fill adds the proceeds `|q|·P` to cash and raises `reserved` by `|q|·P·(1 + c)`, so net `buying_power` falls by `|q|·P·c + costs` — so short sales too fill only within `buying_power`, by the same allocation rule as buys (the actual cost function, proportional scaling): a short-sale candidate's `need_i(q) = round_minor(c·q·P_i) + Σ round_minor(cost component_i(q))` (exactly the change in `buying_power` after the fill) [R3-09]. Longs have no margin loan, so the book's feasible region is `long_weight + c·short_weight ≤ 1` (3.7, E16 before the run) [R3-08] — the rule of the cash account (default); with `[account] type = "margin"`, 5.6.7a instead of this section [R4G-MG-02]. A cover releases collateral and raises `buying_power` (costs excluded). Long buys only within `buying_power`. When the price moves so that `buying_power < 0` (short prices rise), there is no forced liquidation (no model, assumption sentence); new short sales and long buys are blocked (`blocked(collateral)`), and the number of such sessions and the largest shortfall are reported as `CollateralWarning` (E38). Blocked new risk is not ruin by itself (ruin is `decision_value ≤ 0`) [R3-09]. With no shorts, `reserved = 0`, so it is the same as v3 (INV-22).
- **Exit orders and cash** [R4F-06] (decision point C; ruined sleeves included): long exits (sells) raise cash, so they fill as they are, and in the same phase they fill **before covers**, so their proceeds are used for covers (the existing order). Covers (short exits included) fill only within usable cash `max(cash, 0)` (INV-06 as it is: order fills do not create or grow negative cash), and exit quantity not bought for lack of cash is **not removed** but carries over as `blocked(cash)` to the next phase or session (keeping the "no expiry" of exit orders, 5.6.2a; only rebalance buys get `qty_cancelled(insufficient_cash)`). When dividends, interest or long sell proceeds come in, it fills in the next phase by the same rule. In the report's orders line, the number of `blocked(cash)` exits and sessions. Test G-94.
- **Buy cash allocation** (the same rule in all three fill phases; the state at the start of a phase is as in 5.6.3 [R3-10]) [RV-C06]: sells (long sells → covers) fill first and cash is updated (covers spend cash but release collateral; covers among themselves are allocated within `max(cash, 0)` by the rule below, and the unbought part of exit covers is `blocked(cash)` above [R4F-06]). Then short sales and long buys are allocated within `buying_power` by the same rule [V31-LS-04]. For each buy candidate, fillable quantity `f_i = min(remaining_i, the remaining part of the cap)`. Amounts are computed with the **actual cost function**: `need_i(q) = round_minor(q·O_i) + Σ round_minor(cost component_i(q))` (exactly the formulas of 5.6.8 — minimum commission, nonlinear impact `(q/ADV)^a2` and rounding included). (1) `k = min(1, cash / Σ need_i(f_i))`, `q_i = floor_lot(k·f_i)`. (2) If `Σ need_i(q_i) > cash`, **starting from the candidate with the highest fill ratio `q_i/f_i`** (ties by ascending `instrument_id`), reduce by one unit and recompute `need_i`, repeating until it fits (`need` is monotone in q, so it ends; instead of v2's descending `instrument_id` rule, under which a particular id always lost out). The part not bought for lack of cash (`f_i − q_i`) is removed from the order by the quantity event `qty_cancelled(insufficient_cash)` (`cancelled_qty` increases). Leftover cash is not redistributed to other instruments [design 3.6 "proportional scaling", 11.4.2, fix of A-09]. In the report's orders line, the count and amount of `insufficient_cash`.
- Intraday-phase buys use the same rule within **the cash at the start of that phase** (intraday sell proceeds excluded), and close-phase buys within the cash at that point.
- **Order fills** never take cash (`cash_minor`) below 0 (cash account; except the `zipline` preset, 5.6.15; test G-41; for the margin account, 5.6.7a). **Mandatory obligations** (borrow cost, short dividends, short cash in mergers and spin-offs, terminal obligations, `[taxes] pay = "cash"` payments) are booked as they are even when cash is short and can make cash negative (event column `shortfall = true`, number of sessions and largest shortfall in E38; user decision 2026-10-28) — obligations are not dropped or kept as hidden payables. Negative cash in the cash account bears no interest (assumption sentence "no margin interest on debit balances"; the margin account has `debit_rate_bps`, 5.6.7a). With negative cash, `buying_power < 0`, so new short sales and long buys are blocked [R3-09]. v1 settlement is the **immediate-settlement research model** (sell proceeds are usable in that session at once; it does not claim to match broker cash accounting) [11.4.1]. This assumption is visible in the config too, through the reserved key `[settlement] model = "immediate"`, and is recorded in the manifest's `policies.settlement`. US T+1 and Korean T+2 models are revisited when daily-turnover strategies are used [SC-18, OQ-13].

#### 5.6.7a Margin account (`[account] type = "margin"`) [user decision 2026-10-28, R4G-MG-02~08]

The default is `"cash"`, and then 5.6.7 holds as it is (output bit-identical, P-MARGIN-OFF). With `"margin"`, the following changes the cash rules of 5.6.7. Counted separately per sleeve (no loans between sleeves).

- **One formula** [R4G-MG-05]: equity `E = cash + Σ_i q_i·mark_i` (long +, short −; receivables and unsettled entitlements included = 5.6.9 `decision_value`). Requirement `R(ρ) = Σ_i |q_i|·mark_i·ρ[m_i]` (the same formula for longs and shorts; short proceeds are in cash, so it means the same as Reg T's "150% of short value"). Initial requirement `IM = R(initial_margin)`, maintenance requirement `MM = R(maintenance_margin)`. The cash account's short collateral `reserved` and `collateral_ratio` are not used in the margin account (this formula replaces them; if present in the config, they are ignored with a 1-line notice).
- **Buying power** [R4G-MG-05]: new long buys and short sales only as far as they keep `excess = E − IM ≥ 0`: the candidates' `need_i(q) = round_minor(initial_margin[m_i]·q·P_i) + Σ round_minor(cost_i(q))` are allocated within `excess` by the same allocation rule as 5.6.7 (actual cost function, proportional scaling, one unit at a time from the highest fill ratio). And gross exposure `Σ|q|·mark ≤ max_gross·E`. So the gross exposure cap = `min(max_gross, 1/initial_margin_max)`·E (with several markets, conservative with the largest `initial_margin`). Fills can make cash negative (that is the loan). Long sells and covers reduce the requirement, so they always come first (the 5.6.7 order as it is).
- **Feasible region** [R4G-MG-02]: 3.7's `long_weight + c·short_weight ≤ 1` (130/30 → E16) applies only to the cash account. In the margin account, exceeding `long_weight + short_weight ≤ min(max_gross, 1/initial_margin_max)` is E16 before the run ("margin book exceeds buying power: L + S = {x} > {cap}"). 130/30 passes with `initial_margin` 0.5 and `max_gross ≥ 1.6`.
- **Loan interest** [R4G-MG-04]: loan `debit = max(0, −(cash − Σ_short |q_i|·mark_i))` (short proceeds are taken as not used to repay the long loan — the existing `short_rebate_bps` applies to short proceeds). At session start, the previous session's closing `debit × debit_rate_bps/1e4 × calendar days/day_count` is booked as the event `margin_interest` (a mandatory obligation, negative cash allowed), leg `financing` row (INV-06). Positive cash interest (`cash_rate_bps`) only on the part where `cash − Σshort value > 0`.
- **Margin call** [R4G-MG-06]: in the session's close phase (after `close_mark`), if `E < MM`, the event `margin_call` (E, MM, shortfall) is recorded, and in **the next session's open phase** forced reduction orders are sent: all holdings are reduced by the same ratio, keeping the fraction `f = clip((E − Σ_i cost_i(q_i·(1 − E/MM)))/MM, 0, 1)` (on the close valuation, computed once; costs are subtracted in advance so that E ≥ MM after the reduction and the call does not repeat every day) [R4H-02, R4H-03]; the reduced quantity is `min(|q_i|, ⌈|q_i|·(1 − f)⌉ units)`, **never more than the holding** (the sign does not flip). If `E ≤ 0`, `f = 0` — all holdings are liquidated and that sleeve is marked `ruined` (the same decision point as the ruin rule), risk-reducing groups first (long sells → covers, the 5.6.7 order), market orders, filled without the allocation rule or buying-power check (reduce only); halted instruments carry over per 5.6.5, and the remaining sleeve is not reduced again. Fills are the event `forced_liquidation` (instrument, quantity, price), rebalance orders are `CANCELLED(margin_call)` in that session, exit orders are resized to the remaining holding. Checked again at that session's close (if still short, repeated at the next open). The report header line "margin calls: {n} sessions, forced liquidation {x}" and an E38 summary.
- **Ruin** [R4G-MG-07]: the 5.6.7 rule as it is (`decision_value ≤ 0` → `ruined`); in the margin account the same check is made even without shorts when there is a loan. Forced reduction comes after the ruin check (a ruined sleeve gets only exit and forced-reduction orders).
- **Money management** [R4G-MG-08]: 5.5.1a's "no leverage `Σ|w| ≤ min(1, max_gross)`" is a cash-account rule. In the margin account, `G_max = min(max_gross, 1/initial_margin_max)`, and the k of `size.vol_target` can exceed 1 up to this `G_max` (the fourth term is `1/(initial_margin_max·Σ|w|)`). Other sizers stay as they are (≤ 1 per side); only `vol_target` and the L and S of `long_short` scale up.
- **Assumption sentence**: "margin account: single requirement R(ρ)=Σ|q|·mark·ρ; margin call checked at close, forced proportional reduction at next open; no intraday margin calls; rules-pack margin seeds unverified".

#### 5.6.8 Costs [R-25, R-61, 11.4.2]
The fill price is always the raw base price (O, C, Lp, Sp). Every cost is a separately recorded **cash deduction** (not put into the price — no double counting) [11.4.2]. Fill amount `N = q·P` (local currency). The sensitivity multiplier κ (0, 1, 2) multiplies every component.

| Profile | Components |
|---|---|
| `provisional` (default) | `provisional = κ·N·b_side/1e4`, `b` = toml `provisional_bps`[market][side] |
| `bps(buy, sell)` | `other = κ·N·b/1e4` |
| `per_share(c, min)` | `commission = κ·max(min, q·c)` |
| `realistic` | `commission = κ·max(min_amount, N·b_comm/1e4)` (table, that date·market·side), `tax = κ·N·b_tax/1e4` (sells), `other = κ·N·b_other/1e4`, `spread = κ·N·hs/1e4`, `impact = κ·N·Y·σ_t^a3·(q/ADV_t)^a2` (default a2 = 0.5, a3 = 1 → square-root impact `Y·σ·√(q/ADV)`) |
| `none` | 0 |

- `hs` (½ spread, bp) = `clip(½·S_t·1e4, min_half_spread_bps[market], max_half_spread_bps)`. `S_t` = the Abdi–Ranaldo (2017) estimate: for each pair j of consecutive sessions in t−21..t−1, `η_j = (ln H_j + ln L_j)/2`, `c_j = ln C_j`, `s²_j = 4(c_j − η_j)(c_j − η_{j+1})`; `S_t = sqrt(max(mean(s²), 0))`; with fewer than 10 pairs or without H and L (4.5 F-01), `hs = min_half_spread_bps`, and the number of such fills is counted as `spread_floor_fills`. The Corwin–Schultz and Roll estimates are not used for engine costs, only for diagnostic comparison in `bench/` [blueprint chapter 8 item 4].
- `σ_t` = standard deviation of daily log returns over t−20..t−1 (ddof=1), `Y = impact_y` (0.5), `a2 = impact_a2`, `a3 = impact_a3`. Changing these knobs changes `settings_hash`, making a new candidate (attempt count +1, 6.2). The impact formula is a special form of Kissell I-Star and leaves a path to calibrate it later with live trading records (`decision_price`, `arrival_price`) [SC-11].
- Amount rounding: once per fill amount and per cost component, to an integer of the currency minor unit (`minor_unit`, KRW 1, USD 0.01), by that component's rule in the market rules pack `[rounding]` (default half-up, 5.6.1) [V31-05]. The value before rounding is kept in the event too. Cash entries are integer sums and so independent of order [11.4.4, RV-C07]. Nautilus documented that "rounding rules differ by clearing house and exchange" (CME, Eurex), and the truncation practice of Korean taxes and commissions [unverified] is overridden by the user.
- **Effective-price diagnostic** [V31-14]: for every fill, `effective_price = P ± (½ spread + impact)/q` (buy +, sell −) is recorded in the event, and the count and share of those outside that session's [L, H] are given in the report's cost line (the price is not changed; a signal that order sizes are unrealistic. backtrader clips slippage to inside the bar range).
- **Provisional cost table** (optional) [V31-27]: with `provisional(table=…)`, `b_side` is looked up in the table row of (market, side, fill-date interval, ADV-amount interval at the decision); without a row, E23. By default there is no table.
- The profile name is written in the report header, with "(provisional costs)" appended for `provisional` [R-61].

#### 5.6.9 Sleeves, currencies, valuation [R-62, 11.4.1, C5]
- **Initial funding**: start time `inception_ts` = the run's first decision `decision_ts` (the earliest across all sleeves). Base-currency capital K is divided by `[sleeves] weights`, and sleeves in other currencies are converted once at the last exchange rate with `ts_available <= inception_ts` (minor-unit rounding). After that there is no cash movement between sleeves [11.4.1].
- Sleeve values (local currency):
  - `decision_value(d) = cash + Σ qty·mark + Σ receivables and payables of known amount` (used for decision sizing; qty is signed). Every value that goes in here is **a value knowable at `decision_ts`**: `mark` = the raw close of `row_m(d)` (if absent, the stale valuation price of 5.6.3 [V31-02]; that bar's `ts_available <= decision_ts`), receivables = those whose amount was public before `decision_ts` (dividend `ts_terms_available`, terminal recovery `ts_value_available`, correction entries reflected). Entitlements of unknown amount (pending dividends, terminal entitlements whose recovery is not public) are left out [RV-C01]. On the short side, **obligations of unknown amount** (short dividends whose amount is not public, delistings of shorted instruments whose recovery is not public) are not left out but put in as a **rule estimate**: a short dividend is that instrument's previous dividend amount of the same kind (0 if none observed, E30 count), a terminal obligation is `short_exit_estimate = q·ref*·(1 + max(ret_hi, 0))` (q < 0, `ref*` is from 5.6.13; the rule name is recorded in the manifest). Per TDS 5.13, `ret_hi` is a scenario, not an upper bound, so it is not called "the largest burden" [V31-LS-05, R3-09].
  - `nav_economic = decision_value − Σ short rule estimates + Σ after-the-fact realised amounts of entitlements and obligations of unknown amount` (signed: long entitlements +, short obligations −; used for report metrics; not used for decisions) [11.4.3 (5), R3-09]. An estimate is **replaced** by the after-the-fact amount, not added to (the v3.1 formula added the after-the-fact obligation once more on top of the short estimate: with cash 1,100 and estimate −100, `decision_value` 1,000, and with after-the-fact −80, `nav_economic` = 1,020; v3.1 gave 920). After-the-fact amounts are future data and never enter sizing, orders or cash (test G-42, P-FUT).
- **Combined valuation**: valuation time `v` = the same time as the `decision_ts` rule of the schedule's calendar session (with `market_local`, the close of the base-currency market). Combined NAV (base currency) = Σ sleeve `nav_economic` (the last valuation of that sleeve with `ts_close <= v`) × exchange rate (the last observation with `ts_event <= v`; after-the-fact allowed for reports). If the exchange rate is older than `max_stale_days_valuation`, warn and use the last value.
- The drift of country weights (sleeve value / combined value) is recorded and reported every day [11.4.1].
- **Currency attribution** [SC-07, OQ-3]: sleeve s's daily return in the base currency `R_s = (1 + r_local,s)(1 + r_fx,s) − 1` is split exactly into `r_local,s` (local-currency return) + `r_fx,s` (change of the valuation exchange rate, 0 for the base-currency sleeve) + `r_local,s·r_fx,s` (cross term), and the combined return = `Σ_s w_s(previous day's combined NAV weight)·R_s` is built up. Recorded per date in `attribution.parquet` (5.9.1). No approximation (the sum is checked by the test `test_fx_attribution_sums`).
- **Annual linking** [RV-C07]: component CAGRs do not add, so annual values are defined as follows. For sleeve s, annual: `L = Π(1 + r_local) − 1`, `F = Π(1 + r_fx) − 1`, cross = `R_year − L − F` (= `L·F` + linking residual; the row sum of the additive table matches the annual return — v3.1's `(1 + R) − (1 + L)(1 + F)` was always 0 when R was linked exactly) [R3-20]. Annual contributions of the combined portfolio: the daily contributions `c_{s,k,t} = w_{s,t}·component_{s,k,t}` are summed, and the difference between the daily sum and the annual combined return is shown as one row `linking_residual` (so row sum = annual combined return, exactly). The cost attribution rows are linked the same way.
- **NAV and P&L bridge** (INV-06) [RV-C07]: a `bridge` row every day per sleeve and currency: `price_pnl` (Σ holding × change in valuation price, including valuation against the fill price; shorts have negative quantities, so a price rise is a loss), `distributions` (entries of dividends, return of capital, cash consideration and fractional-share cash; **short dividend payments are negative** [V31-LS-05]), `action_corrections`, `costs` (per order cost component, negative), `financing` (borrow cost, rebates, cash interest — borrow cost moved from v3.1's `costs`: an account event without an order, so it is not mixed into the execution-cost comparison) [V31-LS-04, R3-09, R3-20, R3-25], `external_flows` (ledger deposits and withdrawals, 0 in backtests) [V31-19], `fx` (currency exchange and exchange-rate effects, only in base-currency aggregation), `rounding` (minor-unit rounding residual) — sum = `nav_economic_t − nav_economic_{t−1}`, difference 0 (minor units). As columns in `daily.parquet`, checked by P-BRIDGE. Tests G-48 (valuation gain without trades: only `price_pnl`, and the bridge closes), G-49 (the bought quantity changes because of commission).

#### 5.6.10 Splits, reverse splits, stock dividends [11.4.3 (3)]
At the start of the effective session (quantity events whose terms are published late, 5.3.3a(6)), ratio ρ = the resolver's share factor `q_e` = the exact ratio `ratio_num/ratio_den` (required in TDS v1.4; if absent E13, or, with the user's choice `decimal_ratio = "literal"`, the decimal notation of `share_factor` (if absent, only for split-type events, the `ratio_new_per_old` rule — `stock_dividend` and `bonus_issue` are `1 + ratio_new_per_old`) [R4F-01]; the kind mapping is the table in 4.6; the cash of a paid capital reduction separately as a receivable) [RV-C03]:
- Holding `q' = sign(q)·((|q|·num) div den)`; the fractional share `frac = ((|q|·num) mod den)/den` (5.6.1 integer arithmetic [V31-01]) is received as cash `sign(q)·frac·prev_close_raw/ρ` (minor-unit rounding) (`cash_in_lieu`; money paid if short [V31-LS-05]). Examples: 16 shares 1:8 → 2 shares, fraction 0; 7 shares 1:3 → 2 shares + ⅓ share in cash; −7 shares 1:3 → −2 shares − ⅓ share in cash (G-53, G-69).
- `avg_cost' = avg_cost/ρ`, `high_water' = high_water/ρ`.
- Remaining orders: `remaining' = floor_lot(remaining·ρ)` (integer arithmetic), `limit/stop' = price/ρ` snapped to the grid again (5.6.4a). The quantity accounting is recorded as the event `order_rebased(ρ)`, together with `requested`, `filled` and `cancelled` converted by the same ρ (the fractional rounding difference goes to `cancelled_qty`; INV-18 kept). Exit orders are resized per 5.6.2a(3).
- When a stock dividend or bonus issue occurs on a short holding, the short quantity grows by the same ratio (the rights of borrowed shares are repaid to the lender; the lock on new shares does not apply to shorts) [V31-LS-05].
- New stock-dividend shares (`b·q`) are not put into `sellable_qty` until `pay_date` when `bonus_shares_tradable = at_pay_date` (immediately if there is no pay date) [A-16].

#### 5.6.11 Cash dividends [11.4.3 (4)(5)(6), R-60]
- **Entitlement**: created at the start of ex-date session t for the holding q as of the previous close (not the holding on the pay date). The entitlement or obligation quantity is fixed by this holding — even when the terms are published late, and even if it is sold or covered in between (5.3.3a(4)) [R3-01]. **If q < 0 (short), it is an obligation**: amount `q·D` (negative, the full pre-tax amount without withholding — a substitute dividend), taken from cash on the pay date through the same entitlement, receivable and payment path (event `dividend_short`) [V31-LS-05]. Leaving out short dividends as LEAN and zipline do inflates short returns. The short obligation for a dividend with `gross_or_net = "net"` needs the pre-tax amount: if `[dividends] withholding[market] = w > 0`, restore it as `D_gross = D/(1 − w)` with an E30 count; if w is 0 the pre-tax amount cannot be determined, so `AccountingDataError` (E13, "short dividend obligation needs a gross amount") [R3-09].
- **Amount**: `D` = `cash_per_share` (raw amount, TDS). If the terms time `ts_terms` (5.3.3a) is at or before `ts_effective`, immediately a receivable `q·D·(1 − withholding)` (only for q > 0; withholding 0 when `gross_or_net = "net"`; q < 0 follows the obligation rule above [R3-09]); otherwise it is kept as an entitlement of unknown amount and turned into a receivable at the start of the first session after the publication time.
- **Payment**: cash at the start of the `pay_date` session (`pay_date_status = observed`; a date the producer gave as `estimated` is also used as it is, but counted as `estimated = true`). Without a pay date, `missing_pay_date = "estimate"`: estimated pay date = `ex_date + median(pay_date − ex_date)` (over observed events of the same country and the same kind whose ex-date is within `estimate_window_years` years before this event and whose `pay_date` is earlier than this event's ex-date, in calendar days); if the observations are fewer than `estimate_min_obs`, `fallback_pay_lag_days[country]`. It is paid in the first session on or after that date and marked and counted as `estimated = true`. With `"fail"`, `AccountingDataError` (error E13) [9.0 Q29, R-60, A-08].
- Withholding `withholding` (default 0) is recorded separately as the event `dividend_tax`. `return_of_capital` is processed by the same path without withholding (the event kind is recorded as it is). Capital-gains tax is 5.6.11a. With the default 0, "dividends pre-withholding" in the report header.
- **Corrections and cancellations** [RV-C03]: when the resolver issues a different version (amount correction) or `cancelled` at a later reference time, the correction entry of 5.3.3a(7): before payment, the receivable amount is corrected (`action_correction`); after payment, a cash correction (the same event kind); for a cancellation, a reversing entry of the receivable. The entitlement quantity (the ex-date holding) is not changed. Tests G-43 (amount correction after payment), G-44 (cancellation after announcement).

#### 5.6.11a Tax scenarios [OI-18, SC-17, user decision 2026-10-28]
- **Principle**: taxes, like commissions, **are written by the user in the config file** (3.11 `[taxes]`). The engine has no built-in per-country tax rates or systems and computes the written values as they are. Default `profile = "none"` (no taxes, "pre-tax" in the report header). Transaction taxes (securities transaction tax, SEC fee, etc.) are not here but in the cost table (5.6.8).
- **Dividends**: withholding is `[dividends] withholding` (5.6.11) as it is. Progressive calculations such as comprehensive taxation are not done (written in the report's assumption line).
- **Capital gains**: for every sell fill, realised P&L = fill amount − costs − acquisition cost (`basis`: average = moving average, fifo = first bought first). For shorts, for every cover fill, realised P&L = short-sale proceeds − short-sale costs (in proportion to the covered quantity, **only once**) − cover amount − cover costs (the same basis rule, average short-sale price) [R3-20], and it goes into the same group sum (short gains are taxed too, short losses offset too) [V31-LS-05]. P&L is converted to `[taxes] currency` at that day's valuation exchange rate and recorded as the event `realized_gain`. For each year (1/1~12/31) it is summed per group (losses offset within the same year, no carry-forward across years), and `max(0, sum − annual_exemption) × gains_rate` is set as that year's tax (integer minor units, rounding rule 5.6.1).
- **Payment**: with `pay = "report"`, NAV is not changed, and `daily.parquet` gets `nav_after_tax` (the value minus the taxes determined and accumulated up to that day) and `tax.parquet` (year, group, realised P&L, exemption, tax). With `pay = "cash"`, it is taken from the sleeve cash of that group's market at the start of the first session of `pay_month` in the next year (event `tax_payment`; when a group spans several markets, split by realised P&L share; booked as negative cash even when cash is short — a mandatory obligation, 5.6.7 [R3-09]). Taxes still unpaid at the end of the run stay in `tax.parquet` as unpaid and are reflected in `nav_after_tax`.
- **Limits (documented)**: loss carry-forward across years, major-shareholder rules, comprehensive taxation, foreign tax credits and tax years that differ by country are not done in v1. When needed, the user approximates them with group tax rates.
- Tests: G-51 (hand calculation of average acquisition cost, exemption and loss offset), G-52 (`pay = "cash"`, deducted in May of the next year).

#### 5.6.12 Mergers, rights, unsupported events [11.4.3 (9)]
- `stock_merger`, `mixed_merger`: at the start of the effective session, holding q of the old instrument → the new instrument `sign(q)·((|q|·num) div den)` (ratio `ratio_num/ratio_den`; if absent E13, or the decimal literal the user chose, 5.6.1 [V31-01, R4F-01]) + cash `q·cash_per_share` (mixed merger; paid if short) + fractional-share cash (`sign(q)·frac·the new instrument's previous close`). A short holding becomes a short in the new instrument [V31-LS-05]. The average purchase price carries over the total amount. Orders on the old instrument are cancelled. If the new instrument is not in `instruments` or is in another sleeve (another country), `AccountingDataError`. A `delistings` row this event handled (`handled_by_event_id`) does not apply the terminal value again.
- `cash_merger`: at the start of the effective session, a receivable `q·cash_per_share` (on the pay date or in that session), holding removed, orders cancelled.
- `rights_issue` (rights of a rights offering): with `[corporate_actions] rights = "lapse"` (default), the rights are neither exercised nor sold and **the holding quantity is unchanged** (share factor 1); the event `rights_lapsed` (estimated value `q·prev_close·(1 − price_factor)`, not put into the account) is recorded and the report shows the count and the sum of estimated values (G-47). With `"fail"`, `UnsupportedCorporateActionError` (error E12) [A-15].
- `spinoff`: with `new_instrument_id` and `ratio_new_per_old`, it gives `sign(q)·((|q|·num) div den)` shares of the new instrument (if short, a short in the new instrument is created too), and the old holding stays as it is [V31-01, V31-LS-05]. Without them, it is an unsupported event.
- `symbol_change`, `name_change`, `par_change`: no accounting effect, only the event is recorded.
- When an unsupported event (`exchange_offer`, `other`, a spinoff with missing information, etc.) occurs on a **held instrument**, with `unsupported = "fail"` (default) it stops with error E12 (the instrument is not quietly deleted or kept as a fake). With `"cash_at_adjustment"`, cash `q·prev_close·(1 − price_factor)` (if `price_factor` is null this policy cannot be used either, E12) is put in that session and marked with the event `approximated` [11.4.1, 11.4.3].

#### 5.6.13 Delisting [Q18, 11.4.3 (7)(8)]
- The input is TDS `delistings` (without an event row, 4.5 F-13). Terminal session x = the first session in that instrument's calendar on or after `exit_date`. From after `last_trading_date` until before x, the holding is not tradable and is valued at the last valid close (`stale`).
- At the start of x: that instrument's orders `CANCELLED(delisted)`, holding removed, a **terminal entitlement** created (for a short, q < 0, so a **terminal obligation** — the lower the terminal value, the larger the short's gain [V31-LS-05]). The amount is one of **three bases**, depending on what r (table below) is [R3-05]. Two reference prices: `ref_units` = `ref_price` × Π (the price factors of **share-unit events** (split, reverse split, stock dividend, bonus issue, consolidation) that took effect from after `ref_date` until before x — matching the quantity unit only), `ref*` = `ref_units` × Π (the price factors of **cash distributions** in the same interval; the same rule as the stale valuation price of 5.6.3) [V31-02]. q is the holding in that unit (splits already reflected by 5.6.10).
  - (a) **Observed, inclusive** (r = `ret_observed`, `ret_observed_includes_distributions = true`): terminal = `q·ref_units·(1 + r)` − (the sum of those cash distributions already booked for that instrument after `last_trading_date`, the same sign rule). The observed return is relative to `ref_price` (TDS 5.13), so distribution factors are not multiplied in, and distributions already booked are subtracted. The result is not cut even if negative (needed to match the inclusive total).
  - (b) **Observed, exclusive** (r = `ret_observed`, flag false): distributions are booked separately per 5.6.11, and terminal = `q·ref_units·(1 + r)` (the observed amount excludes distributions, so the distribution factors are **not multiplied in** — multiplying would reduce it twice by the distributions).
  - (c) **Policy value** (r = `ret_policy`, `ret_lo`, `ret_hi`, or the 0 of `observed_only`): distributions are booked separately, and terminal = `q·ref*·(1 + r)` (the capital left after distributions is the policy's basis).
  - (d) **Observed, inclusion unknown** (flag null and there is an ex-date distribution after `last_trading_date`) [R4F-05]: no guessing. With `[delisting] unknown_inclusion = "conservative"` (default), of the two readings (a) and (b), **the one that makes less sleeve value** is used (long = (a), with the smaller total recovery; short = (b), with the larger total to repay), with an E30 count + the event `inclusion_assumed(conservative)`; counting distributions twice (v3.3's "treated as false": with `r = 0`, 151.00 + 151.30 = 302.30) does not happen for longs. With `"error"`, the economic calculation of that holding and label stops with E13 (Sol's proposal as an option). The majority of the three reviewers (Opus, Fable) said "proceed on the conservative side + warn" and Sol said "stop", so the default is the majority proposal and the stricter one is left to configuration.
  - So an observed 0 (b) and the policy `observed_only`'s 0 (c) give different amounts when there are distributions. This is intended: the vendor's observed value is based on the last trading price, and the policy on the remaining capital.
- **Distributions just before delisting only once** [V31-04, R3-05]: a cash distribution (`cash_dividend`, `special_dividend`, `return_of_capital`) whose ex-date is after `last_trading_date` and before x is booked **as known at the ex-date** through the entitlement, receivable and payment path of 5.6.11 (the event `distribution_in_terminal` marks the subtracted term of (a)), and an inclusion flag learned later (known time = `ts_value_available`) does not retroactively erase distributions already booked — the subtracted term of (a) fixes the terminal amount (append only, INV-16; v3.1's "if inclusive, do not book distributions separately" made the past be rewritten by a flag not yet known at the ex-date). If the column is null and such a distribution exists, (d) (conservative side + E30, paired with the TDS-A011 warning) [R4F-05]. G-55 prevents LEAN#9819 (delisting liquidation with liquidation distribution 15.10 + stale price 15.13 → counted twice).
- **When the amount is known** [RV-C01]: if r is `ret_observed`, the amount becomes known at `ts_value_available` (if null `ts_exit_known`, and if that is null too, x's `ts_open`, TDS 5.13). Until then the terminal entitlement is an **entitlement of unknown amount** (the same path as a pending dividend: excluded from `decision_value`, the after-the-fact amount only in `nav_economic`), and it becomes a receivable at the start of the first session after the publication time. If r is a policy value (`ret_policy`, `ret_lo`, `ret_hi`, or the 0 of `observed_only`), the engine policy sets that value at x, so it is a receivable of known amount from the start of x. Payment = `max(pay_date, the first session after the amount is published)` (if pay_date is missing or earlier than x, the first possible session on or after x).
- Test G-42: even when rows of an October delisting + a December recovery publication are appended to the snapshot, the `targets` and `orders` of the October and November decisions are byte-identical (a fixed case of P-FUT).

| `policy` | observed | estimated / unobserved |
|---|---|---|
| `observed_then_policy` (default) | `ret_observed` | `ret_policy` (error E13 if null) |
| `observed_only` | `ret_observed` | 0 (last price) |
| `lo` | `ret_observed` | `ret_lo` |
| `hi` | `ret_observed` | `ret_hi` |

- **One shared terminal-value function** [R4F-04]: (a)~(d), the policy table and the amount publication time are computed only by the one function `terminal_value(q, held_path, delisting_row, policy, as_of) -> (amount, ts_known, basis)`, and accounting (this section), the label `fwd_return` (5.8), the shadow index (5.5.2a) and the triple-barrier label (5.8) all call this function (no separate formulas; `tests/unit/research/test_labels.py::test_terminal_uses_shared_resolver`). The label path passes as `held_path` the quantity starting from 1 share (splits and stock dividends reflected) and the distributions that path has already received (reinvested); a distribution with no valid price to reinvest at (ex-date after the last trading day) is carried as cash.
- The terminal value is applied only once (when the reference price `ref_price` is a Korean liquidation-trading price that already contains the loss, the reference producer gives `ret_observed = 0`).
- A short holding's terminal obligation of unknown amount is taken in `decision_value` as the rule estimate `short_exit_estimate = q·ref*·(1 + max(ret_hi, 0))`, and replaced in `nav_economic` by the after-the-fact amount (5.6.9) [V31-LS-05, R3-09].
- The scenarios `delist_lo` and `delist_hi` (included in the default scenarios) rerun the same targets with the `lo` and `hi` policies and report the CAGR range. The policy id (`policy_id`) and the usage count are recorded in the manifest.

#### 5.6.14 End of run [11.4.1]
It stops after the close valuation of the last session. No forced liquidation. Remaining orders (`final_open_orders`) and unpaid receivables are written in `metrics.json`.

#### 5.6.15 Preset `zipline` (tests only) [R-55, 8.1]
`preset="zipline"` is used only in `tests/parity` and `nx.backtest(..., preset="zipline")`, and writes "parity preset (not a realistic model)" in the report header. It cannot be set as a default in toml.

| Knob | zipline preset value |
|---|---|
| Fill time and price | the **close** of the session after the decision, price = C·(1 ± 5bp) (buy +, sell −) — FixedBasisPointsSlippage(5, 0.1) |
| Information time | `information_lag_sessions = 1`: the decision's signal row = `row_m(d) − 1` (zipline `before_trading_start` has data up to the previous day), sizing price = the close on the decision day |
| Volume cap | 10% of that bar's volume (sum of all orders on the same instrument), remainder carried over (NeverCancel) |
| Commission | PerShare(0.001, min 0), no currency distinction |
| Tradability | blocks zero volume only. Halt and limit rules off |
| Cash | negative allowed (zipline has no cash constraint) |
| Quantity | like `order_target_percent`, `int(target value/price)` at the close at the decision (truncated toward 0; signed short targets toward 0 too) |
| Delisting | settled at the last price (the same as `observed_only`) |
| Limit and stop trigger and fill | the zipline 3.2.1 way: the trigger is judged on the bar's **close alone** (`check_triggers(close)`), and the fill price is the close plus slippage (`fill_rule = zipline_close`) [R7-12: in the pinned tag 3.2.1, `finance/slippage.py` lines 169 and 184, the trigger price = `data.current(asset, "close")` was confirmed (2026-10-29); the fill-price side is still an estimate — T3.15b reads `finance/execution.py`, `finance/order.py` (trigger state) and `finance/slippage.py` at the pinned 3.2.1 and confirms or fixes this row; the three-reviewer review confirmed the same direction on upstream main, but that does not replace evidence from the pinned version; OQ-9] |
| Fill record | `fill_resolution = "1d"`, `fill_rule = zipline_close` |
| Dividends | entitlement at the ex-date, cash on the pay date, withholding 0 |
| Cost sensitivity and scenarios | off |
| Tie-breaking | `tiebreak = "id"` (zipline sid order) [V31-29] |
| Price grid and rounding | grid snapping off, rounding half_up [V31-05] |
| Small-order filter and that day's volume cap (ψ) | off (the same-bar 10% replaces them) [V31-06, V31-03] |
| Shorts | negative holdings allowed (zipline style), borrow cost 0, no collateral constraint (the same meaning as allowing negative cash), short dividends are paid [V31-LS-11] |

### 5.7 Metrics [R-38, 11.4.5]

Valuation series: the combined `nav_economic`, daily (valuation calendar); the per-sleeve local-currency series use the same formulas.

| Metric | Formula |
|---|---|
| Daily return | `r_t = NAV_t / (NAV_{t−1} + F_t) − 1`, `F_t` = external deposits and withdrawals that came in at the start of that session (ledger only; 0 in backtests, so the same as v3's `NAV_t/NAV_{t−1} − 1`). Time-weighted return (TWR) [V31-19] |
| CAGR | `(I_T/I_0)^(365.25/D) − 1`, D = calendar days between the first and last valuation dates, `I` = the TWR cumulative index (`I_t = I_{t−1}·(1 + r_t)`, `I_0 = 1`). In backtests external flows are 0, so it equals `NAV_T/NAV_0`. With the NAV ratio, a ledger's CAGR rose from deposits alone (v3.1) — the growth of account value is separately `account_growth = NAV_T/NAV_0 − 1` [R3-19]. With a ruined sleeve, "n/a (ruin)" (5.6.7) |
| Volatility | `std(r, ddof=1)·√252` (annualisation fixed at 252, recorded in the manifest) |
| Sharpe | `mean(r − rf)/std(r − rf, ddof=1)·√252`, risk-free rf = 0 (cash interest `[account] cash_rate_bps` (default 0) is already in the return r and is not subtracted as rf; report header "cash earns {r}%") [RV-Q02, R3-25]. If `std = 0`, "n/a (zero variance)" |
| Sortino | `mean(r)/sqrt(mean(min(r,0)²))·√252` |
| Maximum drawdown | `min_t(NAV_t / max_{u≤t} NAV_u − 1)`; the first value of the valuation series = the initial funding K at `inception_ts` (NAV_0), and it counts in the peak calculation (a first-day loss is a drawdown too; qlib#2358) [V31-23]. With deposits and withdrawals, the TWR cumulative index instead of NAV |
| Turnover (one rebalance) | `(½·Σ_i |w_i^after − w_i^before| + ½·|cash^after − cash^before|) / max(gross_before, gross_after, 1)` (weights within the sleeve, cash included; short weights by the difference of signed values [V31-LS-10]; the denominator normalises only long–short books whose gross exposure exceeds 1, and long-only keeps the v3.1 value — "target turnover including residual cash", not the actual fill volume) [R3-20] |
| Turnover / year | sum of turnover / elapsed years (combined by a sleeve-value-weighted mean) |
| Cost drag / year | `CAGR(gross) − CAGR(net)` = direct costs + path effect (the effect of quantities and holdings that differed because of costs). Direct costs = annual mean of the cost-row sum / mean NAV (per component), path effect = the rest. The report writes the two separately [RV-C07] |
| Gross return | **one series of the same targets replayed separately with zero costs (κ=0)** (the same series as `cost0x`, replayed only once) — not net return + cumulative costs [11.4.5, RV-Q02] |
| Cost sensitivity | CAGR and Sharpe replayed for each κ ∈ `sensitivity` (κ = 0 reuses the gross replay, κ = 1 is the main run) |
| Sub-periods | CAGR, Sharpe and MDD for each 10 years from the start |
| Holding count | median of the number of held instruments on valuation days (per sleeve) |
| Benchmark | a combined series (base currency) that invests in each index with the same initial weights as the sleeves and lets them grow separately. Built on **the same dates at the same valuation time v** as the portfolio valuation series; first value = initial weight × K (the valuation at `inception_ts`), and the first return of both is on the next valuation day (the one-day offset of zipline#1832) [V31-15]. The report header writes the index name, TR/PR and weighting method (TDS v1.2 `instruments.index_weighting`; if absent "weighting unknown") together with the portfolio's weighting method (qlib#2315: −4% a year from the difference between an equal-weighted portfolio and a cap-weighted index alone). n/a if absent |
