| Relative to benchmark [V31-15] | `excess_t = r_t − r^bench_t` (same valuation calendar), annual excess return both arithmetic (`mean·252`) and geometric (`(Π(1+r)/Π(1+r^b))^(252/T) − 1`) under different names, tracking error `std(excess, ddof=1)·√252`, IR = arithmetic annual excess / tracking error, maximum drawdown of the cumulative excess series. n/a without a benchmark. α·β regression later |
| Monthly table [V31-15] | calendar-month return = the compounding of that month's daily returns `Π(1 + r) − 1` (not the sum), a year × month table, benchmark excess at the end of each row |
| Target gap and execution quality [V31-14] | per decision, `target_gap = ½·Σ_i |w_i^held − w_i^target|` (when that decision's orders are done or just before the next decision, whichever comes first) and its breakdown by cause (by block reason, cash shortfall, volume cap, lot rounding `rounding_gap` [R3-20], small orders and band, manual instructions); per fill, `slip_vs_decision_bps = side·(fill_price/decision_price − 1)·1e4`, `slip_vs_arrival_bps` (the same form, basis O), reported as median and amount-weighted mean. All derived from the canonical events (INV-16) |
| Exposure [V31-LS-10] | on every valuation day, long, short, gross (Σ|w|) and net (Σw) exposure (within the sleeve), the median on the first screen, per date in `exposures.parquet` |
| Rolling windows and date luck [V31-26] | a table (HTML) of quantiles (5·25·50·75·95%) and counts of the benchmark excess return over 1-year and 3-year windows (start date moved each month); with `offsets=k`, the minimum, median and maximum of CAGR, Sharpe and MDD across the k series |
| PSR(SR*) | `Φ( (SR̂ − SR*)·√(T−1) / sqrt(1 − γ̂₃·SR̂ + (γ̂₄ − 1)/4·SR̂²) )`, SR̂ = daily (non-annualised) Sharpe (ddof=1), γ̂₃ skewness, γ̂₄ **raw kurtosis (normal = 3; not excess kurtosis)**, both population moments `m_k/m_2^{k/2}` (no bias correction), T = number of daily observations. The implementation allows only the scipy `kurtosis(fisher=False, bias=True)` form, pinned by hand calculation G-60 (putting in excess kurtosis makes the denominator wrong by −0.25·SR̂² for normal returns, so PSR looks larger: LEAN `Statistics.cs:200-214` [est.], vectorbt#872) [V31-07] [Bailey·López de Prado 2012/2014; formula confirmed by three reviewers]. Not computable: T < 30, zero variance, radicand ≤ 0 → "n/a ({reason})" (E21) [RV-C12] |
| DSR | `PSR(SR*_N)`, `SR*_N = sqrt(V[SR]) · ((1 − γ)·Φ⁻¹(1 − 1/N) + γ·Φ⁻¹(1 − 1/(N·e)))`, γ = 0.5772156649, N = the number of distinct strategy candidates recorded within the **cohort (6.2)**, V[SR] = the variance (ddof=1) of the daily Sharpe of each candidate's representative observation (the most recent observation) in that cohort. With N = 1, SR* = 0 (DSR = PSR(0)), shown as "(1 candidate)". The report records `cohort_sha256` [C10, 6.3, RV-C12] |
| MinTRL(SR*, α=0.05) | `1 + (1 − γ̂₃·SR̂ + (γ̂₄ − 1)/4·SR̂²)·(Φ⁻¹(1−α)/(SR̂ − SR*))²` daily observations, divided by 252 for years. SR* = 0. If `SR̂ ≤ SR*`, "n/a (SR ≤ 0)" [SC-09, Bailey·López de Prado 2012 S-23 unverified] |
| Autocorrelation and Lo Sharpe | `ρ1` = the first-order autocorrelation of daily net returns. If `|ρ1| > 0.1`, the Lo (2002) corrected annualisation: `SR_ann = SR_daily · 252 / sqrt(252 + 2·Σ_{k=1}^{q}(252 − k)·ρ_k)`, q = 10 (ρ_k is the sample autocorrelation; k > 10 **truncated** to 0 — the original formula is k = 1..251), shown on the first screen as "lags ≤ 10" [SC-09, S-28 unverified, RV-Q02] |
| Drawdown duration, recovery duration, worst month | calendar days from peak → trough of the maximum drawdown, calendar days of recovery from trough → peak ("not recovered" if it does not recover), the minimum calendar-month return (compounded) [SC-09]. **Recovery = NAV at or above the previous peak** (equality included, compared as minimum-unit integers); peak date = the last day before the trough on which it was at or above the previous peak (backtrader PR #528: counting only "above" as recovery joined two drawdowns together) [V31-23] |
| Signal–weight correlation `signal_weight_corr` | per decision and sleeve, `c_d = corr( zscore(score_d), w_d − w̄_d )` over all instruments in the universe with finite scores (target 0 included), Pearson correlation, not average ranks, summed in the 5.3.10 order. Reported as the median and count across decisions. There is no risk adjustment, so it is not called the Grinold–Kahn transfer coefficient (TC) (renamed from v2's "transfer coefficient") [SC-08, RV-Q02] |
| Currency attribution | annualised contributions (local, FX, cross) of the 5.6.9 formula [SC-07] |
| Capacity ("capacity" scenario) | the same targets replayed with capital ×10 and ×100 (execution only): CAGR, the share of orders hitting the cap (`capacity_bound / number of orders`), mean participation rate (`fill_qty / ADV`). "cap binds at ≈ K" = the capital at the multiple where the share hitting the cap first exceeds 5% (among ×1, ×10, ×100; "> x100" if none) [SC-06] |
| N_eff, DSR(N_eff) | v1.x [SC-10]: the number of clusters from grouping the daily net-return correlation matrix of study candidates with distance `1 − ρ`, average-linkage hierarchical clustering + maximum silhouette (the method name is recorded). DSR by default keeps N [OQ-6] |

### 5.8 `evaluate` computation [R-37, 11.4.5]

- **Decision grid**: per market, the last session of `freq` (market_local). Value = the term value of that row. Sample = universe true ∧ factor finite ∧ label finite.
- **Label `fwd_return`** (economic label, default) [RV-C04, RV-D2]: entry and exit times by `label_entry` (`"next_open"` default: entry = the open of the session after the decision, exit = the open of the session after the next decision; if `horizons` is an integer h, the open of entry + h sessions; with `"close"`, decision close → next decision close). Value = **the holding return of buying 1 share and holding it with dividends reinvested at the ex-date close** (the CRSP-style total-return convention): the label-only factor `m^lab_e = P_e / (P_e + D_e)` (e = ex-date session, `P_e` = that session's raw close, `D_e` = the resolver's cash distribution per share; return of capital included), and quantity events change the share count by the resolver's `q_e`, so `R = (Π_{e ∈ (entry, exit]} (q_e / m^lab_e)) · (P_exit / P_entry) − 1`. Withholding 0 ("pre-withholding"), rights lapses as price effect only (no rights value), and a distribution whose amount is not available at the label computation time makes that label missing (`no_terms`) without E21. Example: bought at 100, dividend 10, ex-date close 100 → 10% (the adjusted-price ratio convention gives 11.1%) — a zero-cost accounting run under the same conditions as test T-21 (a G-19 variant) gives the same value. If it terminates before the exit, the last term is obtained with the shared function `terminal_value` of 5.6.13 (the same bases (a)~(d) as accounting, inclusive-distribution subtraction, unit adjustment, policy; v3.3's independent formula `ref_price·(1+r)` was removed), and ex-date distributions after the last trading day are not reinvested but added as cash — the same value as the 1-share path of a zero-cost accounting run (G-55 (g)) [R4F-04]. If the entry open is missing (halt), it is excluded and counted as `no_entry`. If the exit open is missing, it exits at the last valid close and is counted as `stale_exit`.
- **Label `fwd_return_adj`** (for comparison): `R = (X_exit/C_total[exit]) / (X_entry/C_total[entry]) − 1` (adjusted-price ratio; the zipline and alphalens convention — the reinvestment price is the theoretical ex-dividend price, so it differs from the economic holding return). The zipline comparison and the alphalens adapter use this label. The default can be changed with `[evaluate] target`. In a `tds:minimal-daily` package only this one works (4.3).
- **Label times** [SC-20, RV-C04]: every `LabelFrame` has three: `ts_label_start` (the entry instant: with `next_open`, the entry session's `ts_open`; with `close`, the decision session's `ts_close`), `ts_label_end` (the end of the outcome = the exit instant, by the same rule; for a terminal-value exit, the `ts_open` of terminal session x), `ts_label_available` (the first instant that label value can be computed = max(the publication time of the exit price, the `ts_terms_available` of distributions in the window, `ts_value_available` if the terminal recovery is an observed value)). `Label.*` (TDS `label_panels`) has the same three columns too, by the 4.6 rules.
- **Purging**: a label whose `max(ts_label_end, ts_label_available)` is later than the `ts_close` of the last session of that run segment is dropped (a delisting label whose recovery is published after the segment end leaves that segment; test T-22). The validation methods (6.3a), ML training windows (5.3.11) and triple-barrier labels use the same columns. With `embargo_sessions > 0`, that many decisions before the segment end are dropped too [11.4.5] — a different meaning from the splitter's `embargo=` (the AFML meaning: drop training samples **after** the test block, 6.3a) [R4-19].
- **Label `labels.triple_barrier(take_profit=a, stop_loss=b, max_sessions=h)`** [R4-24, SC-20]: entry = the open `P_0` of the session after the decision (`no_entry` if halted). Barriers = upper `P_0·(1+a)`, lower `P_0·(1−b)` (raw prices; corporate actions move the barriers by the same 5.6.2a rule as exit orders), time = the close of the h-th session counting the entry session as 1 (the same **session count** as `exits.time_stop`; the end time differs — the label is the h-th close, while the exit order is created after that close → fills at the next open [R4F-41]). From the entry session, **the 5.6.4 judgement function (the same code)** is called every session: if the open jumps over a barrier, the open; if both are touched in one bar, the adverse side (the lower one). Value `tb_ret` = end price/`P_0` − 1 (distributions included through the label-only factor of `fwd_return`), `tb_side` ∈ {+1 upper, −1 lower, 0 time}; `ts_label_end` = the end session's `ts_close` (`ts_open` for an open gap), `ts_label_available` by the rule above. A delisting ends like the time barrier, with the 5.6.13 terminal value. `evaluate(target=)` and `ml.factor(label=)` take the two columns (`tb_ret`, `tb_side`). Meta-labels and volatility-width barriers (V31-69) are v1.x. **Why it is in v1**: the judgement function, the session count (`time_stop`), the three label times and purging are already in v1, and the only new thing is "each label has a different end time", which the purging formula handles as it is.
- **IC**: Spearman (average-rank) correlation per decision and market. If valid pairs < `min_pairs` (20), that decision's IC is missing [11.4.5].
- **Newey–West t**: IC series `x_1..x_T` (missing removed), `γ_j = (1/T)Σ_{t=j+1}^{T}(x_t − x̄)(x_{t−j} − x̄)`, `σ² = γ_0 + 2Σ_{j=1}^{L}(1 − j/(L+1))γ_j`, `t = x̄ / sqrt(σ²/T)`. `L = floor(4·(T/100)^(2/9))`, and for overlapping labels (h > 1 decision interval), `L = max(L, h − 1)`. L is written in the report [11.4.5].
- **Quantiles**: the 5.4.3 rule, equal-weighted mean label per quantile. Spread `Q_q − Q_1` mean and NW t. Number of monotone steps = `#{j : mean(Q_{j+1}) > mean(Q_j)}`.
- **Turnover (quantile q)** = the mean of `1 − |S_t ∩ S_{t−1}| / |S_t|`.
- **Coverage** = `Σ valid / Σ universe` (numerator/denominator together) [R-11].
- **By year and by segment**: the same statistics by the year and segment of the decision date.
- **Terminal counts**: per quantile, the number of labels whose exit was a terminal value, by status.
- **Naive breadth and expected IR** [SC-08, RV-Q02]: naive breadth `BR_naive` = (median of valid instruments per decision) × (annual decisions = number of decisions / elapsed years), per market. It is not an estimate of the number of independent bets but a naive opportunity count ("naive opportunity count"). `implied_ir = IC_mean · √BR_naive` (the Grinold–Kahn fundamental law; since it assumes independent bets, a sentence saying to read it as an upper bound is output with it).
- **BHY-adjusted p** [SC-09, RV-C12]: from the NW t values of the representative observations of the m factor candidates in the same **cohort** (observations with the same study, `kind = factor`, segment, label name, horizon and freq; 6.2), two-sided p values `p_i = 2(1 − Φ(|t_i|))` are made, and this candidate's value after the Benjamini–Hochberg–Yekutieli adjustment (`c(m) = Σ_{k=1}^{m} 1/k`, the cumulative minimum from the back of the sorted `p_(i)·m·c(m)/i`, capped at 1). Fixed output of the limitation sentence: "m counts candidates recorded in this cohort only". The HLZ t > 3 hurdle mark stays as it is. With `evaluate(term=Mapping)`, each item is a separate candidate.
- The long−short quantile spread is a diagnostic. To carry it into execution, `long_short(top, bottom)` and `[shorting] enabled = true` (5.5, 5.6) [V31-LS-02].
- `evaluate` too, with `save_signal=True`, leaves `signal.parquet` (the same schema as backtest) (the three-step record of signal table → signal analysis → portfolio analysis) [SC-16]. The public signature of `evaluate` has `save_signal: bool = False` (a keyword outside the "common" of 3.8).

### 5.9 Run records [R-30~R-34, C9, 11.7]

#### 5.9.1 Run id and folder
- `run_id = f"{YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS}-{kind}-{name}-{h6}"`. The time is the local time of the computing machine, `h6` = the first 6 characters of sha256(canonical JSON{graph_hash, params, settings, snapshot_semantic_hash}). If it already exists, `-2`, `-3`.
- Writing: everything is written to `runs/.tmp-<uuid>/`; on success it is `rename`d to `runs/<run_id>/`, the files are made read-only (`chmod a-w`), and `SEALED` (content = the sha256 of `manifest.json`) is written last. On failure the temporary folder is deleted and `status = aborted` is recorded in the index. A write failure is a run failure [R-30, 11.7 (3)(4)].
- At start, (snapshot_id, run_id) is put into the index `pins` (the root `PINS/` when configured, 4.2) [11.7 (9), RV-I04].
- **Seal recovery** [RV-I04]: at every open, among `runs/.tmp-*`, those with `status = running` in the index and no process are deleted and marked `aborted`; if `runs/<id>/` exists without `SEALED` (stopped after the rename and before the seal), `SEALED` is recomputed and written, or, if the content hash differs from the manifest, it is moved to `aborted`. Test `test_runstore.py::test_interrupted_seal_recovers` (forced stop at every commit boundary).

```
runs/<run_id>/
  manifest.json            (below)
  metrics.json             summary metrics (the source of the report and the runs table)
  decisions.parquet        DecisionContext rows
  targets.parquet          canonical targets (5.5)
  orders.parquet           order events (canonical columns below) — canonical
  account_events.parquet   cash entries, corporate actions, dividends, terminations, funding, currency exchange, corrections — canonical (schema below)
  positions.parquet        session_date, sleeve, instrument_id, qty(signed), side, mark_price, mark_status(traded|stale|stale_adjusted), value_local, weight
  daily.parquet            valuation_date, scope(ALL/<MARKET>…), ccy, nav_economic, decision_value, cash, positions_value,
                           receivables, unknown_entitlements, ret, cost_commission, cost_tax, cost_spread, cost_impact,
                           cost_other, cost_provisional, sleeve_weight, bridge_price_pnl, bridge_distributions,
                           bridge_action_corrections, bridge_costs, bridge_financing, bridge_external_flows, bridge_fx, bridge_rounding
                           (amount columns are int64 minor units + base-currency float; `bridge_financing` and `bridge_external_flows` are always present from v3.2 (value 0) even without short sales or a ledger —
                           so that the P-SHORT-OFF baseline does not collide with schema changes in later tickets [R3-11])
  scenarios/{gross,cost2x,delist_lo,delist_hi,capacity_x10,capacity_x100}.parquet   daily series with the same columns (only those turned on; cost0x = the single gross file)
  attribution.parquet      scope(ALL/<MARKET>…), period(date or year), component(local/fx/cross/commission/tax/spread/impact/other/provisional/path/linking_residual), value_base, value_local  [SC-07, RV-C07]
  exposures.parquet        valuation_date, scope, sleeve_weight, ccy_weight(json: currency→weight), sector(per scheme@level, within the sleeve), size_quintile, top10_weight, n_holdings, cash_weight  [SC-07]
  coverage.parquet         decision_id, year, market, term, n_present, n_universe, n_excluded_by_reason(json), n_microcap_excluded, n_signal_nan, n_target_not_held, n_na_filled, n_tied_at_cutoff, target_gap, gap_by_cause(json), rounding_gap, n_zero_lot_targets, n_suppressed_small, n_suppressed_band, n_band_entry_skipped, n_held_missing_score, n_unknown_by_leaf(json)  [SC-23, V31-14, V31-25, V31-29, V31-06, V31-17, R3-13, R3-20]
  trades.parquet           trade_id, sleeve, instrument_id, side(long|short), status(closed|open), ts_open, ts_close, qty_max, avg_entry, avg_exit, legs, realized_minor, unrealized_minor(open), costs_minor, distributions_minor, borrow_minor, exit_reason(rebalance|exit:<rule>|delisted|merger|override), sessions_held, mae, mfe(computed without the extremes of the entry and exit bars, marked "daily-bar approximation" [R3-20])  [V31-20]
  offsets.parquet          (only with offsets) offset, cagr, sharpe, mdd, turnover  [V31-26]
  waterfall.parquet        change in Σ|w| per decision × slot (5.5.2a) [R4-09]
  risk_state.parquet       account-rule state (5.5.2a; 0 rows without account rules) [R4-04]
  skips.parquet            skipped instruments, blocks and rules: decision_id, sleeve_id, stage, instrument_id(nullable), block, reason, n (6.6) [R4-17]
  predictions.parquet      (with ml.factor) 5.3.11 [R4-23]
  fits.parquet             (with ml.factor) 5.3.11 [R4-23]
  validation/              (method ≠ linear) split.json, folds.parquet, paths.parquet, path_daily/<path>.parquet, selection.parquet, stats.json (6.3a) [R4-20]
                           columns [R4F-20]: split.json = {method, params, split_hash, groups[{group, first, last}], splits[{split, test_groups}]}; folds = fold, train_first, train_last, test_first, test_last, selected_params_json, is_sharpe, is_cagr, oos_sharpe, oos_cagr, oos_mdd, n_purged, n_embargoed, n_fits; paths = path, cagr, sharpe, mdd, turnover, ruined, n_days; path_daily = session_date, nav, ret_net, ret_gross, group, split; selection = fold, grid_point, candidate_key, is_metric, is_n_decisions, n_purged, n_embargoed, rank; stats.json = distribution, pbo{value, k, n}, psr, dsr, min_trl, degradation{slope, n}, selection_stability
  signal.parquet           (save_signal=True; in evaluate too) decision_id, instrument_id, term, value
  candidates.parquet       (sweep only) candidate_key, date, ret
  source/                  captured source (below)
  report.html
  SEALED
```

`orders.parquet` canonical columns [11.6]: `event_id, event_kind(created|accepted|triggered|fill|qty_cancelled|exit_resized|order_rebased|price_rounded|status), phase, order_id, decision_id, ts_event, session_date, sleeve_id, instrument_id, purpose, order_type, side, tif, limit_price, stop_price, trigger_state, requested_qty, filled_qty, cancelled_qty, remaining_qty, fill_qty, fill_price, currency, fill_value_minor, commission_minor, tax_minor, spread_cost_minor, impact_cost_minor, other_cost_minor, provisional_cost_minor, cost_unrounded, status, reason, snapshot_id, model_version, fill_resolution, fill_rule, decision_price, arrival_price, price_unrounded, capacity_bound_reason, effective_price, outside_range, slip_vs_decision_bps, slip_vs_arrival_bps` [SC-11, RV-C05, RV-C07, V31-03, V31-05, V31-14]. The `side` values are buy / sell / sell_short / buy_to_cover [V31-LS-06].

`account_events.parquet` canonical columns (schema `nexusbt-events/1`) [RV-I04, RV-C07]: `event_id, seq, ts_event, session_date, phase, sleeve_id, instrument_id(nullable), kind(fill_cash|cost|dividend_entitlement|receivable_booked|receivable_paid|action_correction|cash_in_lieu|rights_lapsed|terminal_entitlement|terminal_receivable|funding|fx_conversion|dividend_tax|approximated|ratio_snapped|distribution_in_terminal|external_flow|override_set|override_cleared|user_fill|user_reject|user_trade|dividend_short|borrow_fee|short_rebate|collateral_change|cash_interest|ratio_unsnapped|sleeve_ruined|user_correct), (v3.1 kinds [V31-01, V31-04, V31-18, V31-19, V31-LS-04, V31-LS-05]; v3.2 kinds [R3-06, R3-09, R3-21, R3-25]) event_chain_id(corporate-action chain, 5.3.3a), amount_minor(int64, signed), currency, amount_unrounded(float64), qty_delta(int64), cash_after_minor, ts_known(the instant the amount became known), shortfall(bool; a mandatory obligation exceeded cash and made it negative [R3-09]), snapshot_id, reason`. A given (sleeve, seq) is unique, and `seq` increases without gaps.

- **Events are canonical** [INV-16]: `orders` and `account_events` are canonical, and `positions`, `daily`, `metrics`, `attribution` and `exposures` are built from the canonical tables (+ snapshot prices and exchange rates) by `artifacts.derive_tables`. The test `test_derived_from_events` (P-DERIVED) checks rebuilt values = stored values.
- Run outputs are only Parquet, JSON and HTML (no pickle, 2.6).

#### 5.9.2 `manifest.json`
Adds the following to the columns of design 3.8: `schema_version: "nexusbt-run/2"`, `decision_contexts_hash`, `clock`, `sleeves`, `markets{key: {calendar_id, currency, rules_pack: "KR@1" | "untested"}}`, `segment`, `segments_touched`, `snapshot_semantic_hash`, `snapshot_value_hash`, `snapshot_physical_hash`, `policies` (the snapshot's `tds.json` policies + engine policy versions: `fill_model: "daily-ohlc-adverse-v1"`, `cost_model`, `cost_table_sha256`, `delisting`, `dividends`, `corporate_actions`, `settlement`), `sim_config` (**every knob value** of appendix C, defaults included) [SC-11], `data` (`tds_version`, `profiles` (the three results), `capabilities`, `unchecked`, `fallbacks`, check level and warning-code counts, `verified_stamp`; 4.4), `assumptions` (a list of sentences: "immediate settlement research model", "capacity = lagged ADV approximation", "cash earns {r}%", "dividends pre-withholding", "intraday sale proceeds usable from close phase", "fill quantity capped by realized session volume (ex-post bound)", "longs fully cash-funded; no margin loan", "no margin interest on debit balances", "stops fill at the stop price unless the open gaps through it", "untested market rules: …" etc.), `retention` ("managed" | "unmanaged", 4.2) [R3-17], `resolved_defaults`{`clock`, `base_ccy`, `sleeves`} [R3-18], `candidate_key`, `observation_id`, `cohort_sha256`, `study_id`, `validation{method, split_hash, n_folds, n_paths}` (when method ≠ linear; the hash of P-SPLIT-DET) [R4F-21], `trial_counts{runs, candidates, observations, independent_assumed}`, `outputs{file: {physical_sha256, value_sha256}}`, `environment{python, platform, cpu(e.g. arm64/Apple M4), machine_id(2.6), uv_lock_sha256, packages{name: version}, numerics{numba_fastmath: false, numba_parallel: false, blas(np.show_config summary), thread_env{OMP_NUM_THREADS, OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS, VECLIB_MAXIMUM_THREADS, NUMBA_NUM_THREADS}}}` [SC-26]. `created_at` and `created_by` (git user name) are left out of the logical hash [R-31].

#### 5.9.3 Capturing source [11.7 (2), C9]
In `source/`: the entry file, every file inside the project folder among the modules imported during the run (paths kept), `nexusbt.toml`, the resolved parameters `params.json`, a copy of the engine `uv.lock`, the project `uv.lock` (if present), and the results of `git rev-parse HEAD` and `git status --porcelain`. `rerun` runs from this source (not from the files of the current working folder) [RV-I04]: (1) it starts a **new Python process** and puts `source/` at the front of `sys.path`, so the captured helpers are loaded before same-named modules of the current working folder (modules already imported in a notebook session do not interfere). (2) If the captured `uv.lock` differs from the current environment, it shows "environment differs" and compares value hashes within tolerance (8.1 L2); to rebuild the same environment, `nexusbt rerun ID --env` creates a temporary virtual environment from the captured lock (`uv sync --locked`) and runs inside it. Test `test_rerun.py::test_archived_helpers_override_local`.

#### 5.9.4 Hashes and determinism [R-31, INV-09]
- Physical hash = file sha256. Value hash = the canonical value serialisation of TDS 4.5 (using the same function `tds.value_hash`; independent of Arrow version and writer). The "logical comparison" of run outputs is the value hash [RV-C10].
- In the same pinned environment (same `uv.lock`, same machine kind), `rerun` must match down to the physical hashes of the outputs. In a different environment, the logical comparison uses a tolerance (the L2 basis of the table in 8.1).
- Parquet writing: pyarrow, zstd 3, row group 131,072 rows, statistics on, string dictionary, no time in the metadata.

#### 5.9.5 Index `runs/index.sqlite` [R-34, 11.7 (8)]
SQLite (WAL). Append only: `BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE` triggers guard the tables below (allowed: `runs.status` running→sealed|aborted, sealed→pruned, filling `pins.ts_released`). The canonical record of pins is this `pins` table (4.2).

| Table | Columns |
|---|---|
| `studies` | study_id(UUID) PK, name UNIQUE, ts_created, hypothesis |
| `runs` | run_id PK, kind, study_id, name, ts_created, created_by, snapshot_id, snapshot_semantic_hash, edition, segment, graph_hash, params_json, settings_hash, candidate_key, status(running/sealed/aborted/pruned), path, metrics_json, derived_from |
| `candidates` | candidate_key PK, study_id, kind(factor/strategy), segment, graph_hash, params_hash, settings_hash, first_run_id (identity only; no statistics columns) [RV-C12] |
| `observations` | observation_id PK, run_id, candidate_key, snapshot_semantic_hash, segment, start, end, target(label name; `net_return` for strategies), horizon, freq, n_obs, sr_daily, skew, kurt, nw_t(factors), method(`linear`/`walk_forward`/`cpcv`/`cscv`), split_hash(nullable), fidelity(`full`/`preview`) [R4F-21, R4F-15], ts_created |
| `cohorts` | cohort_sha256 PK, study_id, kind, segment, target, horizon, freq, members_json(sorted list of (candidate_key, observation_id)), ts_frozen — when a report produces statistics, the cohort of that moment is frozen and stored |
| `exposures` | run_id, study_id, segment, start, end |
| `preregistrations` | id PK, study_id, graph_hash, metric, threshold, segment, note, ts_created |
| `pins` | snapshot_id, holder_id, holder_kind(run/book), ts_created, ts_released |
| `books` | book_id PK, kind(paper|manual) [V31-18], ts_created, base_ccy, sleeves_json, definition_sha256 |

`nexusbt runs --prune` deletes folders and changes only `status = pruned`. Attempt counts do not go down [R-34].

#### 5.9.6 Paper ledger [R-49, 11.8 M5]
- `runs/books/<name>/`: `book.json` (**the unchanging ledger definition**: strategy `graph_hash`, parameter hash, `SimConfig` hash, sleeves, base currency, initial capital, `anchor_decision_id`, engine version, `definition_sha256`), `events/events-<seq>.parquet` (append fragments), `checkpoint-<seq>/` (Arrow IPC of the 5.6.1 arrays + meta JSON), `HEAD` (the last checkpoint name and `state_version`, replaced atomically), `snapshots.parquet` (a record of the `snapshot_id` and semantic hash used at each decision and fill), `book.lock`. Pins of snapshots the ledger used are not released while the ledger exists (4.2) [R3-17].
- **Idempotency key** = sha256(book_id, decision_id, graph_hash, params_hash). `checkpoint_seq` is not put into the key, only kept as an attribute of the order-batch record (v2's key sent the orders of the same decision twice in live → fill → live) [RV-C13]. The ledger holds the set `decisions_emitted` (decision_id → order batch id). For a decision already emitted, `live` shows that batch again (including what has already filled) and does not make a new one (`IdempotencyNotice` E26) [11.8 M5].
- **Transaction** [RV-C13, INV-19]: `live` and `fill` (1) take an exclusive lock on `book.lock` (`fcntl.flock`; if already held, `BookConflictError` E35, no waiting), (2) read the `state_version` of `HEAD` and check that `book.json`'s `definition_sha256` matches the current strategy, parameters, `SimConfig` and engine version (if not, E35 "book definition changed; create a new book or `nexusbt book migrate`(v1.x)"), (3) write the new event fragment to a temporary file and fsync → rename → the new checkpoint folder the same way → replace `HEAD` atomically with `state_version + 1` (temporary → fsync → `rename` → folder fsync) → `status.json`, (4) release the lock. The commit point is the single `HEAD` replacement.
- **Recovery**: when a ledger is opened, event fragments and checkpoints newer than the checkpoint `HEAD` points to (leftovers of an interrupted transaction) are moved to `orphans/` with a warning. So dying at any commit boundary returns to one valid state (the previous or the next) (test P-BOOK: forced stop at every boundary). If the same order batch came from another ledger or another `state_version`, `fill` rejects it (E35).
- At every decision and fill, the `snapshot_id` and semantic hash used then are recorded in `snapshots.parquet` (the input of the 5.9.7 replay).
- `fill` proceeds only when the order's execution session is within the snapshot watermark (`complete_through_session`). Otherwise `DataAvailabilityError` (error E19, CLI exit code 3) [risk 25].
- `fill` calls `execution.step_session` (the same code as the backtest). A run stopped midway becomes the same ledger when restarted from the `HEAD` checkpoint [INV-10].
- **Manual instruction (override)** [V31-18]: `nx.override(book, id, kind)` records the ledger events `override_set`/`override_cleared` (inside a state_version transaction) and does not change the ledger definition (`definition_sha256`). `no_trade` = no new order intents for that instrument, `reduce_only` = no intents that increase size, `close` = only reducing orders at the next decision, as if the target were 0. The target table is unchanged, and the cut happens at the order-intent step (5.6.7) (`order_suppressed(override)`). `reconcile` uses the recorded instructions as replay input (Close / No trading / Reduce only of pysystemtrade `override.py`). Backtests do not have it. **Relation to pending orders** [R3-21]: when an instruction is set, that instrument's unfilled rebalance orders are cancelled to fit the instruction (`no_trade` → all `CANCELLED(override)`, `reduce_only` and `close` → only increasing orders cancelled); exit orders only reduce holdings, so they stay under every instruction.
- **Ledgers a person writes** `manual:<name>` [V31-18]: `live` emits only the order sheet and does not fill automatically. `nx.record_fills(book, csv)` (columns `event_kind, idempotency_key, order_ref, instrument_id, side, qty, price, fees, ts, note` — `event_kind ∈ {fill, reject, correct}` (a discretionary trade is `fill` + an empty `order_ref`), `order_ref` = the order sheet's `idempotency_key`, `idempotency_key` unique within the `manual:<book>:` namespace; a broker fill CSV is converted to this shape and loaded [R3-21]) appends the events `user_fill` (the actual fill of a recommended order; a partial fill is several rows), `user_reject` (the recommendation was not followed — the remaining quantity is cancelled), `user_correct` (a correction of the quantity, price or costs of an earlier `user_fill`, the same `order_ref` and a new key, append only) and `user_trade` (a discretionary trade not in the recommendations) in one transaction (above), and runs only the account accounting (5.6.1, the same code). The same key with the same content is ignored (E26); the same key with different content is rejected (E35, "conflicting duplicate"). This corresponds to Portfolio123 Live Strategy's "rewrite with actual fill prices, reject a recommendation, discretionary mode" and backtrader's `add_order_history`.
- **Deposits and withdrawals** [V31-19]: `nx.cash_flow(book, amount, ccy=, sleeve=)` increases (deposit) or decreases cash at the start of that session (E16 if a withdrawal exceeds cash). The bridge's `external_flows` row; returns are time-weighted (5.7). Deposits and withdrawals can be put only at the start of a session not yet processed (E16 for a session already processed), and the replay makes the same events again at the same boundary; the ledger's CAGR and MDD use the TWR index, and the growth of account value is `account_growth` [R3-19]. Periodic-contribution scenarios for backtests come later (12.1).
- **Order pre-checks** [V31-10]: after emitting the order sheet, `live` runs non-blocking checks and reports through the order sheet column `check`, `warnings` in `status.json`, and `OrderSanityWarning` (E36): (1) the decision price is outside ±`[live] max_price_jump` of the previous valid close, or the close change is outside `spike_sigma × σ_20` (t−20..t−1) (suspected data error; pysystemtrade `max_price_spike`), (2) one order's amount > sleeve value × largest target weight × `max_order_weight_mult` (the per-instrument maximum amount per order of the Nautilus risk engine), (3) long sell quantity > holding, cover quantity > short size, (4) there was an effective event between decisions, but the change in `decision_value` does not match the event factor, (5) the strategy universe has no traded-value threshold leaf, and recent decisions picked instruments in the bottom `liquidity_floor_pct` of ADV (Genport: "live linking is not available unless low-liquidity instruments are excluded"). When some orders are caught by the checks, the CLI exit code stays 0 and they are only marked (a person looks and enters them).
- **Status file** [SC-14]: whenever `live`, `fill` or `reconcile` finishes, `runs/books/<name>/status.json` is written atomically (temporary → `rename`): `{"book", "state_version", "last_decision_id", "last_decision_ts", "last_fill_session": {<MARKET>: …}, "watermark": {<MARKET>: …}, "snapshot_id", "open_orders", "warnings", "last_reconcile": {"ts", "result": "yes"|"NO"|"never", "first_divergence"}, "retention": "managed"|"unmanaged", "ruined": {<MARKET>: date|null}, "ts_written"}` [R3-09, R3-17]. Notifications and scheduled runs (launchd, cron) read this file and the CLI exit codes (0/1/2/3/4) from outside the engine. The engine does not build a scheduler [L5].

#### 5.9.7 Ledger reconciliation `reconcile` [SC-14, INV-10]
1. From the ledger's `anchor_decision_id` to the last processed session, it replays with the same strategy, parameters, sleeves, initial capital and `SimConfig` (the same code as `backtest`, not saved). **Default (`restate=False`, deterministic replay)**: at every decision and fill it uses the snapshot the ledger recorded (`snapshots.parquet`) as it is — E15 if the snapshot is missing. **`restate=True` (restatement)**: every step is recomputed with one `snapshot=` (default latest), and the report labels it "restatement against newer data" (differences may be due to data corrections) [RV-C13].
2. Compared per decision: (a) the canonical target (the final rows of `TargetFrame`, weights exactly), (b) order intents (instrument, side, quantity), (c) fills (session, quantity, price, costs, in currency minor units), (d) per-session `nav_economic` (minor units).
3. If all are equal, `reconciled: yes`. Otherwise it shows the first differing (date, `instrument_id`, stage = targets/orders/fills/nav) and the two values, with exit code 4. Candidate reasons for the difference are shown too: (for a restatement) the snapshot the ledger used differs from the replay snapshot (data correction), a different engine version, ledger events edited by hand.
4. A paper ledger is the same state machine, so the difference must be 0 in a deterministic replay (P-RECON). `reconcile` of a `manual:` ledger produces "model targets vs actual holdings", "model fill prices vs actual fill prices (implementation shortfall bp)" and "the list of discretionary trades" (differences are normal, so exit code 4 is not used) [V31-18]. When a broker ledger exists (11.4), the same report becomes TCA (fill price vs model fill price, implementation shortfall against `decision_price` and `arrival_price`). Broker paper accounts (Alpaca, KIS paper, etc.) are not a verification basis because of random partial fills, unreflected dividends and the like [blueprint 12.2, OQ-10].

#### 5.9.8 Ecosystem adapters (`nx.compat.adapters`, optional pandas dependency) [SC-16]
- **Zero-copy export** [R5-02]: result table objects (`Run`'s `daily`, `trades`, `orders`, `targets`, `attribution`, `PipelineResult`, `FactorReport` tables, `Screen.table`) implement the Arrow PyCapsule interface (`__arrow_c_stream__`). So in `pl.from_arrow(run.trades)`, `pd.DataFrame.from_arrow(run.trades)` (pandas ≥ 3.0) and `duckdb.sql("select * from t")`, **Arrow standard types (integer, float, bool, utf8, date32, timestamp[us, UTC], dictionary-encoded) pass without copying**, and types the receiving side does not support are converted by that library's rules (for example the type conversion of Polars `from_arrow`) — zero copy is not promised unconditionally [R5-02, confirmed by Sol]. The engine does not depend on those libraries. The pandas adapters below keep only ecosystem-specific shapes (pyfolio, alphalens). Users who need a thin layer that converts tables into other libraries' formats can use a tool such as Narwhals (not an engine dependency).
- `Run.returns(ccy: str | None = None) -> pd.Series`: combined daily net returns (default base currency), index = valuation date (`DatetimeIndex`, no tz, name `date`). The input shape of pyfolio and QuantStats.
- `Run.to_pyfolio() -> tuple[pd.Series, pd.DataFrame, pd.DataFrame]`: (returns, positions (instrument columns + `cash`, base-currency values), transactions (`amount`, `price`, `symbol` = `instrument_id`, index = fill instant)).
- `FactorReport.to_alphalens() -> pd.DataFrame`: the alphalens `factor_data` shape (MultiIndex `(date, asset)`, columns `factor`, `factor_quantile`, label column names of the `1D` form from `horizons`).
- The adapters only convert our outputs into those shapes. They do not depend on those packages (tests check only the shape). Without pandas, `CapabilityError` (E18, "install nexusbt[pandas]") instead of `ImportError`.

#### 5.9.9 Decision explanation (`nx.explain(strategy, …)`, `Run.why`) [SC-13]
For decision d (on a day not in the schedule, a preview decision for that date), the path of instrument i is produced as a table of steps:

| Step | Content |
|---|---|
| 1 Universe | listing existence, `investable` (row and `reason`), `exclude_microcap` (threshold and i's market cap), the `&`·`|` tree of the user universe Filter expanded with the true/false of each leaf Filter — "dropped here" where it first became false |
| 2 Score | the value of each node of the signal term tree (top to bottom; if missing, which input is NaN) |
| 3 Rank and selection | rank within the sleeve / number of candidates, whether selected, `per` group, tie handling |
| 4 Construction and risk | `pre_risk_weight`, cap (`capped`), risk overlay (`risk_capped`), final weight, (v1.x) whether it stayed because of the hold band |
| 5 Order intent | `decision_value`, price, `T_i`, holding `q_i`, `Δ_i` (needs the account state, so only in `Run.why` or a ledger; `nx.explain(strategy, …)` shows "needs account state") |
| 6 Execution | the order events of the execution session: fill (quantity, price, `fill_rule`, costs) or `blocked(reason)`, expiry, cancellation |

`nx.explain(strategy, …)` recomputes (steps 1~4, and the tradability judgement of the execution session if the snapshot has that session). `Screen.xray()` is the same table with steps 1~4 spread as vectors over all instruments of that decision (3.8; the holding column only with `book=`) [V31-28, R3-21]. `Run.why(id, date)` produces it from the run outputs (`targets`, `orders`, `account_events`, `coverage`, and `signal` with `save_signal=True`) without recomputation. Without `signal`, steps 1 and 2 show "re-run nx.explain(strategy, …) for filter detail".
